  handL_point:'Near point', handR_point:'Far point', handL_thumb:'Near thumbs up', handR_thumb:'Far thumbs up',
  handL_palm:'Near open palm', handR_palm:'Far open palm' };
function sheetLabel(k){
  const type = ED.modelType || 'humanoid';
  if(isMythicModelType(type)){
    const labels={tail:'Tail base / waist joint',tail_mid:'Tail middle segment',tail_tip:type==='mermaid'?'Tail fin / fluke':type==='ghost'?'Ectoplasm tip':'Tail tip'};
    return labels[k] || SHEET_LABEL[k] || k;
  }
  if(isWingedHumanoidType(type) && (k==='wingL'||k==='wingR')) return k==='wingL'?'Left back wing':'Right back wing';
  if(type === 'animal'){
    const labels={head:'Head / muzzle',torso:'Body',tail:'Tail',armL_upper:'Near front leg (upper)',armL_lower:'Near front leg (lower)',handL:'Near front paw',armR_upper:'Far front leg (upper)',armR_lower:'Far front leg (lower)',handR:'Far front paw',legL_upper:'Near hind leg (upper)',legL_lower:'Near hind leg (lower)',shoeL:'Near hind paw',legR_upper:'Far hind leg (upper)',legR_lower:'Far hind leg (lower)',shoeR:'Far hind paw',head_blink:'Head (blink)',mouth_closed:'Muzzle (closed)',mouth_open:'Muzzle (open)',head_sad:'Head (sad)',head_angry:'Head (angry)',head_shock:'Head (startled)',head_laugh:'Head (happy)',mouth_inner:'Inner mouth (behind head)',eye_L:'Open eye (left)',eye_R:'Open eye (right)',eye_closed_L:'Closed eye (left)',eye_closed_R:'Closed eye (right)',brow_L:'Eyebrow (left)',brow_R:'Eyebrow (right)',mouth_hold:'Muzzle (holding grip)',handL_fist:'Near paw (curled)',handR_fist:'Far paw (curled)',handL_point:'Near paw (raised)',handR_point:'Far paw (raised)',handL_thumb:'Near paw (extended)',handR_thumb:'Far paw (extended)',handL_palm:'Near open paw',handR_palm:'Far open paw'};
    return labels[k] || SHEET_LABEL[k] || k;
  }
  if(type === 'bird'){
    const labels={head:'Head (blank face)',torso:'Body (no wings)',tail:'Tail feathers',armL_upper:'Near default wing',armR_upper:'Far default wing',legL_upper:'Near complete leg and foot',legR_upper:'Far complete leg and foot',bird_eye:'Bird eye (open)',bird_eye_closed:'Bird eye (closed)',bird_brow:'Bird eyebrow',beak_closed:'Closed beak',beak_open:'Open beak',wingL_fly:'Near flying wing',wingR_fly:'Far flying wing',armL_lower:'Near wing (legacy lower)',handL:'Near wingtip (legacy)',armR_lower:'Far wing (legacy lower)',handR:'Far wingtip (legacy)',legL_lower:'Near lower leg (legacy)',legR_lower:'Far lower leg (legacy)',head_blink:'Head (legacy blink)',mouth_closed:'Beak (legacy closed)',mouth_open:'Beak (legacy open)',head_sad:'Head (legacy sad)',head_angry:'Head (legacy alert)',head_shock:'Head (legacy surprised)',head_laugh:'Head (legacy happy)',mouth_hold:'Beak (legacy grip)'};
    return labels[k] || SHEET_LABEL[k] || k;
  }
  if(ED.view === 'side' && SHEET_LABEL_SIDE[k]) return SHEET_LABEL_SIDE[k];
  // v324: in the FRONT view the character faces the viewer, so the piece on the viewer's LEFT is the
  // character's own RIGHT arm/hand (and vice versa). Arm and hand names are shown by the character's side.
  if(ED.view !== 'side' && ED.view !== 'back'){
    const m = /^(arm|hand)([LR])(_.*)?$/.exec(k);
    if(m){ const swapped = m[1] + (m[2]==='L'?'R':'L') + (m[3]||''); if(SHEET_LABEL[swapped]) return SHEET_LABEL[swapped]; }
  }
  return SHEET_LABEL[k];
}
const SHEET_FILL = 0.68;      // fraction of the stage height a new model fills (leaves headroom for jumps)
// Alternate textures ("expression swaps"). SHEET_ALTS[base] lists every texture that can stand in for `base`: the blink and
// the expression heads replace the head, the open mouth replaces the closed one, and the fist / pointing / thumbs-up /
// open-palm textures replace the plain hand (NOT the forearm -- the forearm is untouched by a gesture swap now that it
// is its own piece). SHEET_ALT_OF is the reverse lookup, used to skip an alt when iterating parts normally (it never
// gets its own independent slot in the draw order -- it stands in for its base).
const SHEET_ALTS = {
  head:['head_blink','head_open','head_sad','head_angry','head_shock','head_laugh'],
  mouth_closed:['mouth_open','mouth_hold'],
  beak_closed:['beak_open'],
  armL_upper:['wingL_fly'], armR_upper:['wingR_fly'],
  handL:['handL_fist','handL_point','handL_thumb','handL_palm','handL_hold'],
  handR:['handR_fist','handR_point','handR_thumb','handR_palm','handR_hold']
};
const SHEET_ALT_OF = {};
Object.keys(SHEET_ALTS).forEach(b=>SHEET_ALTS[b].forEach(a=>{ SHEET_ALT_OF[a] = b; }));
// The expression heads are complete heads (their own mouth included), so the closed/open mouth pieces are not drawn over them.
const SHEET_EMO_HEADS = ['head_sad','head_angry','head_shock','head_laugh'];
// Given a base key and a character's expression state, returns which key should actually be drawn.
// `parts` is whatever object holds the sheet's known part keys (sh.parts at runtime, ED.arts while
// editing) -- if the alt texture isn't actually part of this particular model, always falls back to
// the base so an expression flag left set on an old/basic model can never blank out a part.
function sheetActiveKey(baseKey, expr, parts){
  if(!expr || !parts) return baseKey;
  if(baseKey === 'head'){
    const emo = expr.emo ? 'head_'+expr.emo : null;
    // Keep expression heads intact; blinking is composited as an eye-only overlay for moods.
    if(expr.eyeState==='closed' && !(emo && parts[emo])) return parts.head_blink ? 'head_blink' : baseKey;
    if(expr.eyeState==='open' && !(emo && parts[emo]) && parts.head_open) return 'head_open';
    if((expr.blink || expr.ambientBlink) && !(emo && parts[emo])) return parts.head_blink ? 'head_blink' : baseKey;
    return emo && parts[emo] ? emo : baseKey;
  }
  if(baseKey === 'beak_closed' && expr.mouth === 'open' && parts.beak_open) return 'beak_open';
  if((baseKey === 'armL_upper' || baseKey === 'armR_upper') && expr.flying){ const fly=baseKey==='armL_upper'?'wingL_fly':'wingR_fly'; if(parts[fly]) return fly; }
  if(baseKey === 'mouth_closed') {
    if(expr.mouth === 'open' && parts.mouth_open) return 'mouth_open';
    if(expr.mouth === 'hold' && parts.mouth_hold) return 'mouth_hold';
    return baseKey;
  }
  const side = baseKey === 'handL' ? 'L' : baseKey === 'handR' ? 'R' : '';
  if(side){
    if((side === 'L' ? expr.fistL : expr.fistR) && parts['hand'+side+'_fist']) return 'hand'+side+'_fist';
    const g = side === 'L' ? expr.gestL : expr.gestR;
    if(g && parts['hand'+side+'_'+g]) return 'hand'+side+'_'+g;
  }
  return baseKey;
}

// Fresh per-character expression state (blink / mouth / fists). `ambientBlink` is the random idle blink; it is
// kept separate from `blink` (driven by the Talk + Blink clip) so the two can never fight or leave the eyes stuck.
function sheetExprFresh(){ return { blink:false, mouth:'closed', eyeState:'auto', fistL:false, fistR:false, flying:false, ambientBlink:false, faceKitHideMouth:false, emo:null, gestL:null, gestR:null, customExpressionId:null, customGestureLId:null, customGestureRId:null }; }

// Shared prompt rules. Every sheet gets the same joint, colour and layout rules so the image AI output stays consistent.
const sheetCanvas = ratio => "Canvas " + ratio + ". Export with a FULLY TRANSPARENT background (a real alpha channel, nothing behind the pieces -- not a solid color, not a checkerboard, not a matte). Keep edges crisp with no background-colored halo. If your tool truly cannot export transparency, use a pure solid BLACK background instead, and draw the character with NO black or near-black outlines or clothing, so the black can be removed cleanly.";
const SHEET_P_ROUNDED = "ROUNDED, BORDERLESS JOINT ENDS (very important, for smooth animation): every piece that joins another piece must END IN A SOFT, ROUNDED CAP at that joint, like a rounded pill or capsule end. Never a flat straight cut and never a sharp corner at a joint. The shoulder end of each upper arm, the elbow end of each forearm, the wrist end of each hand, the hip end of each thigh, the knee end of each shin, the ankle end of each foot and the neck stub of the head are all smooth and fully rounded, reaching a little past the joint so the piece can rotate with no gap or hard edge showing. The torso's shoulder and hip edges are rounded too, and so are sleeve cuffs and trouser hems. At EVERY edge where one piece mates with or overlaps another, omit the black/dark outer stroke completely on that hidden attachment edge; continue the exact local fill colour to the edge. Keep dark outlines ONLY on exposed external contours, never on internal joining edges. No dark seam, border, shadow or halo at a joint.";
const SHEET_P_COLOR = "COLOR CONSISTENCY (no darkening): every piece must use EXACTLY the same colors and the same brightness as its partner. Do NOT darken, shade, tint or dim any piece, including the pieces on the viewer's right. A left and right piece must look identical in color. Flat, even lighting only: no shading tones, no cast shadows, no ambient occlusion, no darker folds or edges.";
const SHEET_P_COLOR_SIDE = "COLOR CONSISTENCY (no darkening): use one set of flat colors with even brightness across the whole character. The far-side pieces (the limbs and hands on the far side of the body) must be EXACTLY the same colors and brightness as their near partners. Do NOT make the far side darker, dimmer, desaturated or shaded, and do not add depth shading to make a far limb look like it is behind the body. Flat, even lighting only: no shading tones, no cast shadows, no ambient occlusion, no darker folds or edges.";
const SHEET_P_RULES = "Rules: no text, labels, numbers, extra pieces or perspective. Same outline weight on every piece. Every piece fully visible and uncropped.";
const SHEET_P_MIRROR = "IMPORTANT -- mirroring: piece 6 must be a true MIRROR IMAGE of piece 3 (not the same drawing repeated), piece 7 a true mirror image of piece 4, piece 8 a true mirror image of piece 5, piece 11 a true mirror image of piece 9 and piece 12 a true mirror image of piece 10. If the right arm or leg is just a copy of the left one, the finished hand or foot will look anatomically backwards on that side.";

If the right arm or leg is just a copy of the left one, the finished hand or foot will look anatomically backwards on that side.";
const SHEET_P_NOMIRROR = "IMPORTANT -- NO MIRRORING IN SIDE VIEW: pieces 6, 7, 8, 11 and 12 are the far-side pieces. Each far piece has the SAME silhouette as its near partner (pieces 3, 4, 5, 9 and 10). Do NOT mirror or flip them. A mirrored hand or foot would point the wrong way.";

The heads and mouths face the viewer's right in profile and are not mirrored either.";
const SHEET_P_NOSLEEVE = "BARE FOREARMS AND HANDS (no sleeve): every forearm piece and every hand piece -- including every fist, pointing, thumbs-up and open-palm version of the hand -- is COMPLETELY BARE skin, with NO sleeve, NO cuff and NO fabric of any kind, even if the character's upper arm and sleeve are long. Sleeve fabric belongs ONLY on the upper-arm piece, and it ends cleanly at the elbow -- nothing carries over onto the forearm or hand pieces.";
const SHEET_P_HANDFRAME = "HAND-GESTURE FRAMING: every fist, pointing, thumbs-up and open-palm piece REPLACES ONLY the plain hand piece -- same wrist position, same size, same framing as the open hand it is based on. The forearm is a separate piece and is never redrawn for a gesture; only the hand and fingers change shape between versions.";
const SHEET_P_MOUTH_EDGE = "MOUTH-PIECE EDGE BLENDING (important): pieces 14 and 15 are only mouth/lower-face patches that sit directly on top of the main head. Their OUTER silhouette must have NO black, dark-brown, or dark outline at all; use the exact same flat skin colour right to the outside edge as the main face so the patch disappears into the face when overlaid. Keep dark lines ONLY for the actual smile/lips/inside of the open mouth. Do not draw a rectangular border around either mouth piece, and do not add a shadow, halo, or background fringe.";

// Angled view: the head/face is drawn straight-on (fully legible for expression swapping, same as the
// front view's head), but the torso and every limb are drawn turned slightly to one side -- a subtle
// 3/4 angle rather than a flat orthographic front. This gives the body some visible depth and volume
// while the face stays perfectly readable. It still parses as a plain front-view sheet (same 10/25
// pieces in the same grid, same left/right pairing), so nothing in the upload/rig pipeline needs to
// change for it -- only this prompt text and the view picker are new.
const SHEET_PROMPT_ANGLE = [

// Turned view: the head/face is drawn straight-on, fully facing the viewer (exactly like the front view's
// head), but the torso and every limb are drawn in a PURE SIDE PROFILE -- not a subtle 3/4 turn like the
// angled view, but the same full profile body as the side view. This is the "face front, body side" look
// (think of a simple explainer-video mascot standing in profile with its face turned back to the camera).
// Because the body is genuinely side anatomy (one near limb, one far limb, no mirroring), this reuses the
// SIDE view's layout, rig and NEAR/FAR part pairing -- only this prompt text and the view picker are new.
// See buildFromSheet(), which maps sheetView 'turned' onto the 'side' layout path the same way 'angled'
// already maps onto 'front'.

The torso and every limb, however, are drawn in a PURE SIDE PROFILE, facing the viewer's right -- as if the body had turned a full quarter turn while the head turned back to look at the viewer. If a reference image is attached, copy its exact design, colors, proportions and art style.",
 sheetCanvas("16:9"),

Crop the head at the exact same zoom as the torso -- the head must never look zoomed in or blown up compared to the body. Every limb hangs straight down in side profile, relaxed, with no swing. /* ===== v356: HALF-NECK SPLIT (Humanoid, Beast Man, Mermaid, Naga / Lamia) =====
   Until v355 the neck existed ONLY as a short connector on the BODY piece, and every head piece (the base head and the four
   expression heads) was a face-only cut that ended at the chin, so the head had no neck of its own to join the body with.
   From v356 the neck is SHARED: the body piece keeps its neck connector (lower half) and every head piece carries a matching
   neck stub (upper half) of EXACTLY the same width, length, shape and colour. These rules are used by the 8 prompts of the
   four model types only (base + face sheet each); Ghost / Fairy / Devil keep their old wording. */
function evNeckSwap(text,from,to){
  if(text.indexOf(from)<0){ try{ console.warn('[v356 neck-stub] pattern not found: '+from.slice(0,70)); }catch(e){} return text; }
  return text.split(from).join(to);
}
/* ===== v358: SAME-WIDTH NECK HALVES + CLEAN BEAST-MAN NECK =====
   v356/v357 still let the AI draw the neck stub on the head wider than the neck connector on the body (flared, bell-shaped
   or, on the Beast Man, wrapped in fur or hair). The two halves are now specified as ONE plain straight-sided column drawn
   twice: the BODY connector sets the master width, the head stub copies it exactly (never wider, never flared), and on the
   Beast Man base sheet both halves are ONE furred neck column duplicated, the body connector an exact copy of the head stub (v360; the Beast Man face-sheet stub rule EV_NECK_BARE_FACE is unchanged). Same rule text feeds all 8 prompts
   (Humanoid, Beast Man, Mermaid, Naga / Lamia, each with a base sheet and a face sheet). */
const EV_NECK_COLUMN = 'a plain, slim, straight-sided vertical COLUMN (a simple cylinder) whose two side edges are perfectly PARALLEL and perfectly VERTICAL from the top of the half to its bottom: NO taper, NO flare, NO bell or funnel shape, NO widening toward the jaw or chin, NO widening toward the collar or shoulders, NO sloping shoulder or trapezius curve, NO curved or bulging side edges and NO neck-muscle or Adam-apple bump';
const EV_NECK_BEAST_BODYFUR_RULE = 'MATCHING NECK FUR (CRITICAL, FIXES THE MISMATCHED-NECK-TEXTURE ERROR): the neck is a plain straight column that is NOT human skin and NOT a different material from the head. BOTH neck halves carry the NECK FUR of the reference character: the fur seen directly under the chin of the head in the reference (its fur colour, any light throat fur, any stripes or markings, and its short fur texture and shading). The neck connector on the BODY piece must show EXACTLY the same colour, markings, fur texture, shading and outline weight as the neck stub on the HEAD piece: it is a copy of it, never a different colour or pattern (for example never orange and striped on one half and cream or white on the other, and never smooth human skin on one half and fur on the other). Do NOT take the neck colour or stripes from the forearms, the sleeves or the clothing. Compare the two halves before export: if they do not look like the same piece of fur, redraw the body connector as an exact copy of the head stub. The fur lies short and flat INSIDE a clean silhouette: both side edges stay plain straight parallel lines with NO fur tufts, NO ruff, NO mane, NO long hair, NO jagged, spiky or fuzzy edge sticking out sideways and NO fur that makes either half wider than the other. The longer fur of the cheeks, chin, beard and mane belongs to the head and stops at the jaw: it never hangs down over, wraps around or flares out along the sides of the neck stub, and no fur ruff or collar-fur surrounds the base of the body connector (the hood or collar sits around its base only as clothing). ';
const EV_NECK_BARE_FACE = 'CLEAN HUMAN-STYLE NECK STUB, NO FUR AND NO HAIR (CRITICAL): although the character is an animal, every stub is drawn like the clean neck of a human anime character: smooth, bare, fur-free skin in ONE plain flat colour (the SAME neck colour as the stub and the connector on the base sheet) with simple smooth cel shading and plain smooth straight side edges. NO fur, NO hair, NO mane, NO ruff, NO tufts, NO fluff, NO jagged or fuzzy edge, NO stripes, NO spots and NO scales on any stub; this overrides every fur and texture rule elsewhere in this prompt. The chin fur, cheek fur, beard, mane or ruff of each head stops at the jaw and chin and never hangs over, wraps around or runs down the sides of a stub. ';
function evNeckSplitBaseRule(adj,col,bare){
  return 'TWO NECK HALVES (CRITICAL, FIXES THE ONLY-ONE-NECK-PART ERROR AND THE WIDER-HEAD-NECK ERROR): the character has ONE neck, and on this sheet that one neck is CUT ACROSS THE MIDDLE INTO TWO HALVES, so there are exactly TWO neck parts on the sheet, one on the head piece and one on the body piece, and BOTH must be drawn. (1) The HEAD piece (part 1) carries the UPPER half: a short '+adj+' NECK STUB hanging straight down from the chin and jawline, centred under the chin. (2) The BODY piece (part 2) carries the LOWER half: a short '+adj+' NECK CONNECTOR standing straight UP out of the collar, clearly visible ABOVE the collar, hood, neckline or shoulder line. Never draw the whole neck on the head only, never draw the whole neck on the body only, and never leave the body piece without its own neck: if the neck in the reference is hidden by a hood, hoodie collar, scarf, mane or high neckline, STILL draw the body neck connector as a visible '+adj+' column standing up out of the collar, with the hood or collar folded down around its base instead of covering it. '
  +'IDENTICAL TWIN COLUMNS (CRITICAL, FIXES THE HEAD-NECK-WIDER-THAN-BODY-NECK ERROR): the two halves are ONE shape drawn twice. Each half is '+EV_NECK_COLUMN+'. Both halves have EXACTLY the same WIDTH, the SAME length, the SAME '+col+', the SAME shading and the SAME outline weight, so neither half is wider, thinner, longer, shorter, darker or paler than the other. '+(bare?'ONE-COLUMN BUILD (Beast Man): draw ONE finished furred neck column ONCE, slim, about as wide as the real neck of the reference and never wider than about 40% of the width of the head at the cheeks, then DUPLICATE it exactly (the same width in pixels, the same length, the same fur colour, markings, texture and shading): one copy is the neck connector standing up out of the collar on the body piece, and the other copy, turned so its cut end points DOWN, is the neck stub under the chin on the head piece. The two halves are literally the same drawing, so the body connector must look EXACTLY like the head stub in every way. ':'The BODY connector sets the master width: it is a slim column about as wide as the real neck of the reference where it leaves the collar, and never wider than about 40% of the width of the head at the cheeks. Draw the body connector FIRST, then copy it exactly (the same width in pixels, the same length) for the head stub, turned so its cut end points DOWN. ')+'The head stub must NEVER be wider than the body connector: if the stub looks even slightly wider, or flares out toward the jaw, redraw it narrower until its two side edges sit exactly on the same vertical lines as the two side edges of the connector. Each half is short, about 1.2 to 1.5 times as long as it is wide (roughly 3 to 4 percent of the image height), and reaches just a little past the middle cut of the natural neck so the two halves overlap when assembled. The stub is perfectly vertical under the chin even if the head itself is turned or tilted, and the connector is perfectly vertical above the collar. ASSEMBLY TEST: when the head is laid over the body, the left and right edges of the stub must continue as the left and right edges of the connector in two unbroken straight vertical lines, with no step, ledge, bulge, narrowing or widening at the join, so the two halves read as ONE continuous neck. '
  +'Each half ends in a clean FLAT cut (a straight horizontal end with only slightly rounded corners, corner radius at most one fifth of the width, so the width does not shrink toward the tip) that carries NO dark outline across it (dark outlines run only along the exposed side edges of the neck), so the two halves join with no seam, gap or dark line. Each half contains ONLY neck: NO collar, NO shoulder, NO chest, NO clothing, NO necklace and NO hair pixels on it, no dark shadow patch and no fade at its end; hair may frame the face and hang beside the jaw but never covers, fills or widens the stub. '
  +(bare?EV_NECK_BEAST_BODYFUR_RULE:'')
  +'Each half is part of its own piece (head or body), NOT a separate piece, so the piece count does not change. On the sheet itself the head and the body stay fully separate: leave the usual clear transparent gap between the bottom end of the head neck stub and the top end of the body neck connector, so they never touch (they only overlap when the model is assembled).';
}
function evNeckStubFaceRule(col,bare){
  return 'HALF-NECK STUB (CRITICAL, FIXES THE HEADS-NOT-JOINING-THE-NECK ERROR AND THE WIDER-HEAD-NECK ERROR): the neck is shared between the head and the body, and every complete head on this sheet (parts 5, 6, 7 and 8) carries the upper half of it. Each of the four heads keeps the SAME short NECK STUB as the head on the base sheet, drawn as '+EV_NECK_COLUMN+'. The stub hangs straight down from the chin and jawline, is centred under the chin, is perfectly vertical even if the head is turned or tilted, and ends in a clean FLAT cut (a straight horizontal end with only slightly rounded corners). The width of the neck connector on the BODY piece of the supplied base sheet is the master width: draw all four stubs at EXACTLY that width, with the SAME length, the SAME shape, the SAME '+col+', the SAME shading, the SAME outline weight, the SAME position under the chin and the SAME angle. If the stub on the base head is wider than the connector on the body piece, or flares out toward the jaw, do NOT copy that flaw: draw the four stubs narrower, at the width of the body connector. All four stubs are identical twins of each other and of the body connector, so none is wider, thinner, longer or shorter than another, and any of the four heads swapped in over the base head still overlaps into ONE continuous neck, its two side edges continuing the two side edges of the connector in unbroken straight vertical lines with no step, ledge, bulge or taper at the join. The flat bottom end of the stub has NO dark outline across it (dark outlines run only along the exposed side edges). The stub contains ONLY neck: NO collar, NO shoulder, NO chest, NO clothing, NO hair pixels hanging below or beside it, no dark shadow patch and no fade; hair never covers, fills or widens the stub. '
  +(bare?EV_NECK_BARE_FACE:'')
  +'Only the four complete heads carry a stub: the inner-mouth layer (part 9), the eyes, the closed eyes, the eyebrows and the hands never contain any neck. The stub is part of each head silhouette, NOT a separate piece, so the piece count stays 19, and the four heads stay fully separated from each other by transparent space, stubs included.';
}

Before export, re-check the four expression heads one by one: each is turned to the same three-quarter angle as the base head (never front-facing or symmetrical) and each keeps the nose in the same place. Export one transparent PNG.'
].join('\n\n');


/* v356: face-sheet copy WITH the half-neck stub on every complete head (Humanoid, Beast Man, Mermaid, Naga / Lamia).
   Derived from SHEET_PROMPT_VARIANTS_16 so the original text stays available to the dormant Ghost / Fairy / Devil builders. */
function evNeckStubVariants(src,ruleAfterHeadMatch){
  let t=src;
  t=evNeckSwap(t,'the gap must pass from the top hair edge to the bottom chin edge.','the gap must pass from the top hair edge to the bottom end of the neck stub.');
  t=evNeckSwap(t,'cheek, jaw, chin or outline may touch','cheek, jaw, chin, neck stub or outline may touch');
  t=evNeckSwap(t,'HEAD MATCH: all four heads are cut at the chin and jawline with NO neck and NO throat piece attached. They keep the exact same crop, size,','HEAD MATCH: all four heads end in the SAME short, plain, straight-sided NECK STUB under the chin as the base head, exactly as wide as the neck connector on the body piece of the base sheet and never flared (see the HALF-NECK STUB rule). They keep the exact same crop, size,');
  t=evNeckSwap(t,'neck or throat attached to any head,','expression head without the neck stub, neck stub missing on any head, neck stub with a different width or length or colour than the base head, neck stub on only some of the four heads, neck stubs of different widths on the four heads, neck stub wider than the neck connector on the body piece of the base sheet, flared or bell-shaped or tapered neck stub, long neck on any head, collar or shoulder or clothing pixels on a neck stub, dark outline across the end of a neck stub, neck on the inner-mouth layer,');
  t=evNeckSwap(t,'four eye-free, brow-free, neck-free expression heads whose open mouths','four eye-free, brow-free expression heads that each end in the identical short, straight-sided neck stub (the same width as the neck connector on the base body piece) and whose open mouths');
  t=evNeckSwap(t,'and each keeps the nose in the same place.','and each keeps the nose and the identical neck stub in the same place.');
  if(ruleAfterHeadMatch){
    const anchor='Preserve an identical eye/brow anchor location across all four heads.';
    t=evNeckSwap(t,anchor,anchor+'\n\n'+ruleAfterHeadMatch);
  }
  return t;
}
/* v390: FACE-KIT FIXES for the six uploadable model types (Humanoid, Girl, Beast Man, Beast Girl, Mermaid, Naga / Lamia).
   (1) Every expression head (sad, angry, surprised, laughing) has a HOLLOW transparent mouth with NO teeth at all, the angry head included.
   (2) Parts 18 and 19 (holding-grip hands) must be drawn in SIDE VIEW, never as the front-facing / thumb-up fists some AI runs produced.
   The original SHEET_PROMPT_VARIANTS_16 text is left as it was (dormant Ghost / Fairy / Devil builders); the fix is applied on top of it. */
const EV_FK_HOLLOW_MOUTH_RULE = "HOLLOW-MOUTH RULE FOR ALL FOUR EXPRESSION HEADS (CRITICAL, FIXES THE PAINTED MOUTH INTERIOR AND THE TEETH): the sad, angry, surprised and laughing heads each have their own open mouth shape, and on EVERY one of the four heads the inner part of the mouth is a true transparent hole (alpha zero) fully enclosed by the lip outline: hollow, empty and see-through, like a window cut through the head, NOT painted black, white, pink, red, dark brown or skin colour. NO TEETH ON ANY OF THE FOUR HEADS: the sad, angry, surprised and laughing heads all have ABSOLUTELY NO teeth, no fangs, no gritted or clenched teeth, no tooth line, no white tooth shapes and no gum. The angry head shows its anger only through the shape of its open mouth (for example a wide snarling or shouting opening); that opening is just as empty and teeth-free as the other three. Inside every mouth opening there is NOTHING at all: NO teeth, NO tongue, NO throat, NO gum fill, NO dark cavity, NO shadow, NO pink, NO black, NO white fill and NO lower-jaw interior; all of that belongs only to part 9, the separate inner-mouth layer that the editor places behind the head. Closed or tight-lipped areas stay as normal skin/lip art. The hollow must be fully enclosed by the lip outline and stay inside the face silhouette.";
const EV_FK_HOLD_SIDE_VIEW_RULE = "HOLDING-GRIP SIDE-VIEW RULE (CRITICAL, parts 18 and 19; FIXES THE FRONT-FACING AND THUMB-UP FISTS): parts 18 and 19 must be drawn in the correct SIDE VIEW (side profile of the hand), never from the front. Do NOT draw them from the front with the knuckles and the four finger fronts facing the viewer, do NOT draw them as upright fists standing vertically on a straight wrist like a front-view boxing fist, and do NOT draw them with the thumb sticking straight up like a thumbs-up fist (that pose belongs to parts 14 and 15 only). Draw each one as a closed gripping fist in true SIDE VIEW, exactly the way a side-view character's hand looks when it grips a handle: the fist is seen from its side, so the four curled fingers appear as a neat stack of rounded finger segments along the front edge of the fist, the thumb wraps across the top and lies over the curled fingers (never pointing up), the back of the hand forms one smooth rounded outline on the other side, and the short bare wrist stub leaves the fist at its lower back edge. The fist is turned sideways and sits roughly horizontal rather than standing vertical, with the knuckle and finger end pointing toward the centre of the sheet and the wrist end pointing outward. The viewer-left fist (part 18) has its fingers and knuckles pointing to the viewer-right and its wrist at the viewer-left; the viewer-right fist (part 19) is its exact mirrored partner, with its fingers and knuckles pointing to the viewer-left and its wrist at the viewer-right, so the two fists face each other. Show exactly four curled fingers and one thumb on each. Keep the same wrist width, skin tone, nail polish, line weight, shading and scale as the other hand parts, with NO forearm, NO sleeve, NO bangle and NO prop. The finished pair must look like the side-view fist pair of a standard character part sheet: two clearly sideways fists facing each other, never two front-view fists and never two upright thumb-up fists.";
function evFkSwap(text,from,to){
  if(text.indexOf(from)<0){ try{ console.warn('[v390 face-kit] pattern not found: '+from.slice(0,70)); }catch(e){} return text; }
  return text.split(from).join(to);
}
function evFkReplacePara(text,startMarker,newPara){
  const a=text.indexOf(startMarker);
  if(a<0){ try{ console.warn('[v390 face-kit] paragraph not found: '+startMarker.slice(0,70)); }catch(e){} return text; }
  let b=text.indexOf('\n\n',a); if(b<0) b=text.length;
  return text.slice(0,a)+newPara+text.slice(b);
}
function evFaceKitFix(src){
  let t=src;
  t=evFkSwap(t,"5) complete SAD head (NO teeth); 6) complete ANGRY head (gritted teeth allowed); 7) complete SURPRISED head (NO teeth); 8) complete HAPPY/LAUGHING head (NO teeth).","5) complete SAD head (hollow mouth, NO teeth); 6) complete ANGRY head (hollow mouth, NO teeth); 7) complete SURPRISED head (hollow mouth, NO teeth); 8) complete HAPPY/LAUGHING head (hollow mouth, NO teeth).");
  t=evFkSwap(t,"18) viewer-left holding-grip hand; 19) viewer-right holding-grip hand.","18) viewer-left holding-grip fist drawn in SIDE VIEW (see HOLDING-GRIP SIDE-VIEW RULE); 19) viewer-right holding-grip fist drawn in SIDE VIEW, the mirrored partner of part 18.");
  t=evFkReplacePara(t,'HOLLOW-MOUTH RULE FOR ALL FOUR EXPRESSION HEADS',EV_FK_HOLLOW_MOUTH_RULE);
  t=evFkSwap(t,"Holding grip has naturally curled fingers and opposing thumb around an invisible handle; do not draw a prop.","Holding grip (parts 18 and 19) is a closed gripping fist drawn in SIDE VIEW, fully described in the HOLDING-GRIP SIDE-VIEW RULE below; do not draw a prop.");
  t=evFkSwap(t,'\n\nNEGATIVE PROMPT: front-facing head','\n\n'+EV_FK_HOLD_SIDE_VIEW_RULE+'\n\nNEGATIVE PROMPT: front-facing head');
  t=evFkSwap(t,"teeth in the laughing head, teeth added by default,","teeth in the laughing head, teeth in the angry head, gritted or clenched teeth, fangs, teeth of any kind on any of the four expression heads, white tooth shapes or tooth lines or gum inside a mouth, dark mouth cavity or tongue or throat drawn inside the mouth opening of a head, teeth added by default,");
  t=evFkSwap(t,'palm lines, palm creases, 16 parts,','palm lines, palm creases, front view of the holding-grip fist, fist seen from the front, knuckles facing the viewer, four finger fronts facing the viewer, upright vertical fist standing on a straight wrist, thumb pointing straight up on a grip fist, thumbs-up style grip fist, front-facing grip hand, holding-grip hands that are not in side view, both grip fists drawn pointing the same direction, unmirrored grip pair, 16 parts,');
  t=evFkSwap(t,"(teeth ONLY on the angry head, none on the sad, surprised or laughing heads)","(NO teeth on any of the four heads, the angry head included)");
  t=evFkSwap(t,"(point, thumb, open hand seen from the BACK, hold)","(point, thumb, open hand seen from the BACK, and a holding-grip fist seen from the SIDE)");
  t=evFkSwap(t,'Export one transparent PNG.',"Also re-check the four mouths: each opening is an empty transparent hole with no teeth, tongue, throat or colour fill, the angry head included. Also re-check parts 18 and 19: each is a SIDE-VIEW gripping fist (never a front-facing fist and never a thumb-up fist), with the pair mirrored and facing each other. "+'Export one transparent PNG.');
  return t;
}
const SHEET_PROMPT_VARIANTS_16_NECKBASE = evFaceKitFix(evNeckStubVariants(SHEET_PROMPT_VARIANTS_16,''));

/* ===== v347: BASE-SHEET + FACE-SHEET PROMPTS FOR THE SIX CREATURE SECTIONS =====
   Mermaid, Naga / Lamia, Ghost, Fairy, Devil and Beast Men now use the SAME proven recipe as the Humanoid and Animal sheets:
   a loose ANATOMY-POSITION layout (never a rigid grid or table), an exact numbered piece list with a count sum, an
   approximate position map, a no-touch gap rule, a solid-piece rule, type-specific rules, a negative prompt and a final
   count check. The importer (sheetOrderMythicAnatomy / sheetOrderWingedHumanoid16 / sheetOrderBeastAnatomy15) reads the
   pieces by their position on the sheet, so the AI never has to hit exact grid cells. */

  canvas:'CANVAS AND TRANSPARENCY: vertical 9:16 portrait, real transparent PNG alpha background. Pixels are either solid art or fully transparent background.',
  head:'complete neutral head with the reference hair and face, a natural CLOSED-mouth expression, and a completely blank eye area with NO eyeballs, irises, pupils, highlights, eyelids or eyebrows; FACE-ONLY CUT: the head ends with a clean rounded chin and jawline, with NO neck, NO throat, NO neck stub, NO skin tab, NO collar and NO clothing pixels hanging below the chin; absolutely NO eyebrows, NO brow lines, NO brow shadows and NO single stray eyebrow anywhere on the face (the brow area is plain skin and hair only)'
};

/* ---------- MERMAID / NAGA-LAMIA / GHOST : 11 pieces (head, body, 6 arm pieces, 3 lower-body pieces) ---------- */
/* v353: NO-NECK HEAD LOCK shared by the Mermaid and Naga / Lamia sheets (base head piece + all expression heads). */
const EV_MYTHIC_NO_NECK_LOCK = 'NO-NECK LOCK (CRITICAL, FIXES THE NECK-ON-THE-HEAD ERROR): every head piece is a FACE-ONLY cut that ends exactly at the chin and jawline, like the neck-free heads of a classic cut-out rig. Directly beneath the chin there is NOTHING: no neck, no throat, no neck stub, no dark or shadowed neck shape, no skin tab, no collar, no back-of-the-neck hair fill, no shoulder, chest or clothing pixels. With long hair, the hair locks may frame the top and sides of the face and hang beside the jaw, but the space straight below the chin between the hair locks must stay FULLY TRANSPARENT: no hair mass, shadow or dark patch may fill or close the area under the chin. function evBuildMythicBasePrompt(c){
  const T=c.word, TM=c.tailMap||['44 - 60%','62 - 78%','80 - 98%'];
  return [
    `Create a production-ready 2D CUT-OUT ANIMATION BASE-BODY SHEET for a ${c.name} (${c.what}) from the attached character image. The attached character image is the ONLY source of truth for identity, hairstyle, face shape, head angle, skin, outfit, accessories, ${c.look}, proportions, outlines and shading. Preserve the exact same view and visual design; do not redesign, rotate, mirror or simplify the character. The upper body is a normal human upper body and the lower body is ${c.lower}: there are NO human legs, thighs, knees, shins, feet or shoes. The base head has a neutral CLOSED mouth and ${c.neckStub?'NO eyeballs and NO eyebrows, and it ends in a short NECK STUB under the chin (the upper half of the neck, an exact match of the neck connector on the body piece)':'NO eyeballs, NO eyebrows and NO neck/throat'}; eyes and eyebrows are supplied as separate face-kit pieces on sheet two.`,
    EV_SHEET_COMMON.canvas,
    c.canvasExtra||'',
    `LAYOUT: exactly 11 separate pieces arranged near the natural positions of one upright character, like a professional cut-out rig reference, in THREE VERTICAL COLUMNS. CENTRE COLUMN (top to bottom): the head at the top centre, the body directly below it, then the three ${T} pieces stacked one under another (root, middle, tip). LEFT COLUMN: the viewer-left upper arm standing free beside the body (never attached to it), the viewer-left forearm beneath it, then the viewer-left hand beneath the forearm. RIGHT COLUMN: the viewer-right upper arm, then the viewer-right forearm, then the viewer-right hand, in the same way. This is an anatomy-position sheet, NOT a regular grid, NOT a 4×3 table and NOT a row-by-row list. Every part must be isolated by transparent pixels with a clear gap and must not touch another part.`,
    `POSITION MAP (9:16 canvas, measured from the top edge; follow the ORDER, the exact numbers are only a guide): CENTRE column: head 3 - 17%, body 19 - 41%, ${T.toLowerCase()} root ${TM[0]}, ${T.toLowerCase()} middle ${TM[1]}, ${T.toLowerCase()} tip ${TM[2]}. LEFT and RIGHT columns (at the outer edges, level with the body and below it): upper arms 19 - 38%, forearms 41 - 59%, hands 62 - 76%.`,
    `EXACT 11 PIECES (1 head + 1 body + 2 upper arms + 2 forearms + 2 hands + 3 ${T.toLowerCase()} pieces = 11): 1) ${c.neckStub?EV_NECKSTUB_HEAD:EV_SHEET_COMMON.head}; 2) ONE central TORSO-ONLY body piece: a SLEEVELESS trunk from the collar through the waist, drawn as in the reference (${c.torso}), with NO arms, NO hands, NO hair and NO ${T.toLowerCase()} attached to it, INCLUDING its own short skin-coloured neck connector rising from the collar ${c.neckStub?'(the LOWER HALF of the neck: it must stand clearly visible ABOVE the collar, hood or neckline and must never be left out or hidden inside the hood; the matching UPPER half is the neck stub on the head piece, drawn at exactly the same width and length)':'(this neck belongs ONLY to the body piece and is never drawn on the head)'}; its bottom edge is a clean, softly rounded waist cut where the ${T.toLowerCase()} attaches; 3) viewer-left UPPER ARM from shoulder to elbow (with the reference sleeve, armlet or bracelet if present), softly rounded at both ends; 4) viewer-right UPPER ARM, same rule; 5) viewer-left bare FOREARM from elbow to wrist, NO hand; 6) viewer-left HAND ONLY, separated at the wrist (a small separate piece: palm, fingers and thumb); 7) viewer-right bare FOREARM from elbow to wrist, NO hand; 8) viewer-right HAND ONLY, separated at the wrist; 9) ${c.p9}; 10) ${c.p10}; 11) ${c.p11}.`,
    `ARM AND HAND RULE (CRITICAL, FIXES THE MISSING-HAND ERROR): there are SIX arm pieces, not four and not five: two upper arms, two forearms and two hands. A forearm piece ends at the wrist and contains NO hand. Each hand is its own small separate piece (palm and fingers only) placed beneath its forearm with a clear gap. Never merge a hand into a forearm, never merge a forearm into an upper arm, and never draw one long straight arm instead of the three pieces.${c.armExtra||''}`,
    `${T} RULE (CRITICAL, FIXES THE MIS-MATCHED ${T}): in the reference the lower body is ${c.lower}, and it must be sliced into exactly THREE stacked pieces with two cross-cuts: never one piece, never two pieces, never four. Draw it exactly as in the reference first (same colours, scales or pattern, width, curve and outline), then slice it into a top section (piece 9), a middle section (piece 10) and a bottom section (piece 11) and spread the three sections apart vertically with transparent gaps, top section first. Each piece keeps the reference scale and orientation: do not straighten, rotate, shrink or redesign any section, and do not draw three small separate ${T.toLowerCase()}s. ${c.tailDetail} ROUND TAIL JOINT CAPS (CRITICAL): the four INTERNAL cut ends (bottom of piece 9, top and bottom of piece 10, top of piece 11) must each have a clearly visible smooth rounded, semicircular / capsule-like attachment cap in the matching tail colour. These are curved, softly convex joint ends, NOT flat square cuts, NOT sharp corners, NOT pointed ends and NOT separate oval joint pieces. Make each adjoining pair the same width and matching curved contour so the rounded caps overlap slightly when assembled, with no gap or hard seam. Keep the cut pixels fully opaque and matching the local tail fill; remove the dark outline ONLY across these hidden internal joining edges. The natural outer tail silhouette and the fin / snake tip / wisp tip remain exactly as in the reference. The top edge of piece 9 is a clean soft cut with no body pixels on it. Do not split a section into scales, fin rays, wisps or decorative fragments.${c.jointExtra||''}`,
    c.tipLock||'',
    c.tailLock||'',
    c.headLock||'',
    `TORSO-ONLY RULE (CRITICAL): the body piece (part 2) must NOT contain any arm, hand, sleeve, hair strand or ${T.toLowerCase()} artwork. Draw only the trunk exactly as in the reference: collar or neckline, chest, outfit details, the neck connector and the waist edge. Its left and right sides end in clean, softly rounded side edges at the armhole line, so the body is clearly narrower than the full shoulder-to-shoulder width of the character with arms. All sleeve fabric, armlets and shoulder caps exist ONLY on the separate upper-arm parts 3 and 4, and all bare arms and hands exist ONLY on parts 5 to 8. Never duplicate an arm or hand on the body piece.${c.torsoExtra||''}`,
    `NO-TOUCH GAP RULE (CRITICAL, FIXES THE MISSING-PIECES ERROR): the sheet is read by counting separate connected silhouettes, so every one of the 11 pieces must be completely surrounded by transparent pixels. Leave a clear transparent gap of at least 3% of the image height between the head and the body, between the body and the ${T.toLowerCase()} root, between the three ${T.toLowerCase()} pieces, between upper arm and forearm, between forearm and hand, and between each arm column and the centre column. If two pieces touch anywhere (hair touching a shoulder, a hand touching a forearm, a ${T.toLowerCase()} section touching the body), redraw them further apart. Final silhouette count must be exactly 11, not 9 or 10.`,
    EV_SHEET_COMMON.solid,
    `ANGLE AND IDENTITY LOCK: all pieces must use the same angles as the attached character. Keep the reference head at its exact natural front or three-quarter angle; keep the body, arms and hands at their original angles. Do not turn the character into a side profile unless the reference itself is side-on. The 11 parts must reassemble into the same full character without changing its silhouette.`,
    c.rules,
    `RIG AND OVERLAP RULES: every joint end is softly rounded, with matching rounded ends at shoulder, elbow, wrist and at each ${T.toLowerCase()} cut. All four INTERNAL tail cut ends use smooth rounded capsule-like caps with matching curved contours; never use a square, flat, pointed or sharply cornered tail joint. No sharp triangular limb tips and no separate oval or crescent joint pieces. Sleeve or armlet fabric belongs only to the upper arms; forearms and hands are bare skin where the reference shows skin. The body piece keeps the real neck connector and ends at the waist. Keep paired parts correctly oriented and do not mirror asymmetric details incorrectly.`,
    `NEGATIVE PROMPT: 9 parts, 10 parts, 12 parts, 4×3 grid, table layout, rows of equal cells, one long straight arm instead of three pieces, forearm with the hand attached, missing hands, hands merged into forearms, arms attached to the body, hair or arms drawn on the body piece, human thighs, knees, shins, shoes, ${T.toLowerCase()} in one uncut piece, ${T.toLowerCase()} in two pieces, ${T.toLowerCase()} in four or more pieces, ${T.toLowerCase()} straightened or redrawn, ${T.toLowerCase()} attached to the body, ${T.toLowerCase()} touching another piece, touching pieces, merged pieces, square internal tail joint cuts, flat squared tail ends, pointed tail attachment edges, dark outline across internal tail joints, detached waist piece, extra pelvis, ${c.neckStub?('extra separate neck piece, '+EV_NECK_NEG_BASE):'extra neck piece, neck on the head'}, eyes on the head, eyebrows on the head, expression variants, gesture hands, ${c.neg}, full assembled duplicate character, background, checkerboard, labels, numbers, text, arrows, grid lines, cell borders, shadows, glow, semi-transparent area, faded edge, ghost layer, watermark, 3D render.`,
    `FINAL CHECK: count the pieces before export: head 1, body 2, upper arms 3 and 4, forearms 5 and 7, hands 6 and 8, then the three ${T.toLowerCase()} pieces 9, 10 and 11: exactly 11 isolated pieces and no others. Three columns: the arms at the left and right edges, the head, body and three ${T.toLowerCase()} pieces down the centre. Every piece is separated from every other by visible transparent space. The hands are separate pieces, the forearms carry no hands, and the body is sleeveless with no arms or hair on it. The ${T.toLowerCase()} is the reference lower body sliced into root, middle and tip, with the tip details only on piece 11. The head has ${c.neckStub?'no eyebrows and no eyes. Count the neck parts: there are exactly TWO neck halves on the sheet, one short stub on the head piece and one short connector standing up above the collar on the body piece, identical in width, length, shape and colour (both plain straight-sided columns with parallel vertical edges, no flare, no taper, and the head stub NOT wider than the body connector), with no collar, clothing or shoulder pixels on either and separated by transparent space on the sheet; if the body piece has no visible neck connector above its collar, redraw it before export':'no eyebrows, no eyes and no neck'}. No facial-expression variants and no gesture hands. Preserve the attached character exactly and export one transparent PNG.${c.finalExtra||''}`
  ].filter(Boolean).join('\n\n');
}

    `POSITION MAP (9:16 canvas, measured from the top edge; follow the ORDER, the exact numbers are only a guide): head 2 - 16%; body 18 - 42%; upper arms 17 - 36% (just beside the body); wings 12 - 44% (the outermost left and right edges, outside the upper arms); forearms 38 - 54% and hands 56 - 68% (under their upper arms); thighs 44 - 62%, shins 64 - 83% and shoes 86 - 99% (centre area, one on each side of the centre line).`,
    `EXACT 16 PIECES (1 head + 1 body + 2 upper arms + 2 forearms + 2 hands + 2 thighs + 2 shins + 2 shoes + 2 wings = 16): 1) ${EV_SHEET_COMMON.head}; ${c.headExtra}; 2) ONE central TORSO-ONLY body connector piece, a SLEEVELESS trunk from the collar through the hem of the top or dress, with NO shoulder-arm pieces, NO sleeves, NO arms, NO hands and NO wings attached to it, INCLUDING its own short skin-coloured neck connector rising from the collar (this neck belongs ONLY to the body piece and is never drawn on the head) AND the hip/waist wedge of the lower garment (trousers, shorts or skirt start) extending from the lower centre as a rounded triangular shape, matching the reference character; 3) viewer-left upper arm with sleeve, from shoulder to elbow; 4) viewer-right upper arm with sleeve; 5) viewer-left forearm from elbow to wrist, no hand; 6) viewer-left hand only, separated at the wrist; 7) viewer-right forearm from elbow to wrist, no hand; 8) viewer-right hand only, separated at the wrist; 9) viewer-left thigh from hip to knee; 10) viewer-right thigh; 11) viewer-left lower leg / shin from knee to ankle, ending in a clean soft rounded edge ABOVE the shoe, with NO shoe pixels; 12) viewer-right lower leg / shin, same rule; 13) viewer-left shoe or bare foot only, complete from heel to toe; 14) viewer-right shoe or bare foot only; 15) ONE complete viewer-left WING as a single connected silhouette; 16) ONE complete viewer-right WING as a single connected silhouette.`,
    `TORSO-ONLY RULE (CRITICAL): the body piece (part 2) must NOT contain any shoulder-with-arm, sleeve, arm, hand or wing artwork.     `WING RULE (CRITICAL): each wing (parts 15 and 16) is ONE complete uninterrupted silhouette from its root to its tip ${c.wingLook}. Draw both wings fully open and flat, as in the reference, at the same scale as the character (each wing no wider than about 30% of the canvas width and no taller than about 32% of the canvas height). The viewer-left wing has its root at its INNER (right-hand) edge and its tip extending outward to the left; the viewer-right wing has its root at its INNER (left-hand) edge and its tip extending outward to the right.     `NO-TOUCH GAP RULE (CRITICAL, FIXES THE 16-PIECE ERROR): the sheet is read by counting separate connected silhouettes, so every one of the 16 pieces must be completely surrounded by transparent pixels. Final silhouette count must be exactly 16, not 14 or 15.`,
    EV_SHEET_COMMON.solid,
    `ANGLE AND IDENTITY LOCK: all pieces must use the same angles as the attached character. Keep the reference head at its exact natural front or three-quarter angle; keep the torso, arms, hands, thighs, shins and shoes at their original angles. Do not turn the complete body into a side profile unless the reference itself is side-on. The 16 parts must reassemble into the same full character without changing its silhouette.`,
    c.rules,
    `RIG AND OVERLAP RULES: every joint end is softly rounded, with matching rounded ends at shoulder, elbow, wrist, hip, knee and ankle. No sharp triangular limb tips and no separate oval or crescent joint pieces. Sleeve fabric belongs only to upper arms; forearms and hands are bare skin where the reference shows skin. The body piece contains NO sleeve fabric, NO shoulder-arm, NO arm, NO hand and NO wing pixels, and keeps the real neck connector and the hip/waist wedge. Each wing has exactly ONE wing bone from its inner root to its outer tip, so do not add joints, feather pieces or a tail. Shoes are separate from shins. Keep paired parts correctly oriented and do not mirror asymmetric details incorrectly.`,
    `NEGATIVE PROMPT: 14 parts, 15 parts, 17 parts, 4×4 grid, table layout, extra wings, only one wing, missing wing, wing split into multiple pieces, feather pieces, wing fingers or joints drawn as separate pieces, wings attached to the arms, wings drawn on the body piece, wing touching the arm or body, sleeves attached to the body, arms attached to the body, hands attached to the body, duplicate arms, duplicate hands, human legs merged into one, shoes touching shins, shoe fused to shin, neck on the head, throat on the head, eyebrows on the head, eyes on the head, expression variants, gesture hands, ${c.neg}, full assembled duplicate character, background, checkerboard, labels, numbers, text, arrows, grid lines, cell borders, shadows, glow, semi-transparent area, faded edge, ghost layer, watermark, 3D render.`,
    `FINAL CHECK: count the pieces before export: head 1, body 2, upper arms 3 and 4, forearms 5 and 7, hands 6 and 8, thighs 9 and 10, shins 11 and 12, shoes 13 and 14, wings 15 and 16: exactly 16 isolated pieces and no others, every one separated from all others by visible transparent space. The two wings stand at the far left and far right edges, each one a single connected silhouette with its root facing the centre. The body piece is a sleeveless trunk with no sleeves, arms, hands or wings on it. The head has no eyebrows, no eyes and no neck. No facial-expression variants and no gesture hands. Preserve the attached character exactly and export one transparent PNG.`
  ].join('\n\n');
}

function evBuildVariantsPrompt(c){
  const FIRST='Create a production-ready 2D CUT-OUT ANIMATION FACE-AND-HAND KIT for the SAME humanoid character shown in the supplied 14-part base-body sheet.';
  const head='Create a production-ready 2D CUT-OUT ANIMATION FACE-AND-HAND KIT for the SAME '+c.who+' character shown in the supplied '+c.base+' base-body sheet.';
  const SRC=c.neckStub?SHEET_PROMPT_VARIANTS_16_NECKBASE:SHEET_PROMPT_VARIANTS_16;
  const body=SRC.indexOf(FIRST)>=0?SRC.replace(FIRST,head):SRC;
  return body+'\n\n'+c.rules.join('\n\n');
}

const SHEET_PROMPT_BEAST_VARIANTS = evBuildVariantsPrompt(EV_VARIANT_CFG.beastman);
const SHEET_PROMPT_MYTHIC_BODY = evBuildMythicBasePrompt(EV_MYTHIC_CFG.mermaid);      // kept for older code paths (Mermaid wording)
const SHEET_PROMPT_WINGED_HUMANOID = evBuildWingedBasePrompt(EV_WINGED_CFG.fairy);    // kept for older code paths (Fairy wording)

/* Registry used by the two copy buttons (base sheet / face sheet) of the model-upload panel. */
const EV_TYPE_PROMPTS = {
  mermaid:{base:SHEET_PROMPT_MYTHIC_BODY, variant:evBuildVariantsPrompt(EV_VARIANT_CFG.mermaid), baseLabel:'Mermaid 11-part base-sheet prompt', variantLabel:'Mermaid 19-part face and hand prompt'},
  lamia:{base:evBuildMythicBasePrompt(EV_MYTHIC_CFG.lamia), variant:evBuildVariantsPrompt(EV_VARIANT_CFG.lamia), baseLabel:'Naga / Lamia 11-part base-sheet prompt', variantLabel:'Naga / Lamia 19-part face and hand prompt'},
  beastman:{base:SHEET_PROMPT_BEAST_BODY, variant:SHEET_PROMPT_BEAST_VARIANTS, baseLabel:'Beast-man 15-part base-sheet prompt', variantLabel:'Beast-man 19-part face and paw prompt'}
};
