If the reference has a green or coloured background, do not copy it; remove it completely. Export a true transparent PNG with an alpha channel (not a flattened image, not a JPG). No cell borders, no labels, no numbers, no scenery, no shadows, no glow, no watermark. Crisp edges and generous transparent gaps. Draw the character a little SMALLER than a normal full-body picture (head about 10% of the image height) so that all 15 pieces fit with wide gaps.\n\nDRESS-MODEL IDEA (READ FIRST): the WHOLE garment, from the neckline and shoulders down to the hem, is ONE single piece: the BODY (piece 2). The legs are drawn separately as six plain furred pieces (2 thighs, 2 shins, 2 paw-feet). In the finished animation the legs are hidden under the garment and only the paw-feet show below the hem (the tail hangs down behind the garment); when the legs walk, the garment hem is stretched to follow them. So the body must be one complete, simple, stretch-friendly garment, and the leg pieces must be plain legs.\n\nLAYOUT: exactly 15 separate pieces on an anatomy-position sheet (NOT a regular grid). HEAD at the top centre. BODY (piece 2) directly below it in the centre as ONE very tall upright piece. Between the head and the body there is ONLY empty transparent space: no neck pieces, no rectangles, no capsules, no shapes of any kind floating in that gap. Just LEFT of the body: a LEG column with THREE separate shapes (viewer-left thigh, shin, foot, stacked top to bottom on one vertical line). At the far-left edge: an ARM column with THREE separate shapes (viewer-left upper arm, forearm, paw-hand, stacked top to bottom). Just RIGHT of the body: the matching LEG column with THREE separate shapes (viewer-right thigh, shin, foot). At the far-right edge: the matching ARM column with THREE separate shapes (viewer-right upper arm, forearm, paw-hand). So the sheet has SIX arm pieces in total, never four, and the four side columns contain 3 + 3 + 3 + 3 = 12 separate shapes, plus the head, the body and the tail = 15. The separate TAIL (piece 15) is not part of any column: put it in the empty bottom-centre area under the body, hanging straight down with its root at the top; if it does not fit there, put it in any other empty area, and draw the whole character a little smaller if needed. Every part is isolated by transparent pixels and touches nothing.\n\nPOSITION MAP (from the top edge; follow the ORDER, the numbers are only a guide): head including its neck stub 2-12%; body including its neck connector 15-72%; arm columns: upper arm 15-26%, forearm 30-41%, paw-hand 46-53%; leg columns: thigh 36-52%, shin 56-70%, paw-foot 74-81%; tail 77-97% in the bottom centre under the body. Between the shapes in every column there is a clearly visible band of empty transparent space (at least 4% of the image height, and at least 5% between each forearm and its paw-hand). Leave the bottom 3% empty. All pieces use ONE consistent scale, as if cut from the same full-body drawing.\n\nEXACT 15 PIECES (1 head + 1 body + 2 upper arms + 2 forearms + 2 paw-hands + 2 thighs + 2 shins + 2 paw-feet + 1 tail = 15): 1) complete neutral ANIMAL head WITH ITS OWN NECK STUB attached to it as part of the same single piece, with the reference ears, horns, mane or crest, muzzle or snout, nose, whiskers, cheek fur, hair (parting, hair ornaments, flowers), bindi or forehead mark, earrings, nose stud, a natural CLOSED-mouth expression, and a completely BLANK EYE AREA made of plain face fur (see BLANK EYE AREA RULE) with NO eyeballs, irises, pupils, highlights, eyelids, eyelashes, eye sockets, white eye patches or eyebrows; below the clean rounded chin and jawline the head carries a short NECK STUB covered in the neck fur of the reference, NOT human skin (see NECK RULE below); the longer fur of the cheeks and chin stops at the jaw and never hangs over the stub; the stub is an exact copy of the body connector and the head ENDS right there (see HEAD STUB LOCK); long hair or mane that falls past the shoulders ends at about collarbone level with a clean natural tip and hangs BESIDE the stub, never in front of it; absolutely NO eyebrows, NO brow lines, NO brow shadows anywhere on the face; 2) ONE BODY piece = the COMPLETE GARMENT BODY WITH BOTH PLAIN SHOULDER CAPS (no sleeves, no arm parts) and its own short, THICK furred neck connector, exactly as wide and as thick as the head's neck stub, never thinner (see NECK RULE, BODY PIECE RULE and SHOULDER AND PALLU RULE); 3) viewer-left upper arm with its blouse/top sleeve, from shoulder to elbow; 4) viewer-right upper arm with its sleeve; 5) viewer-left furred forearm from elbow to wrist, with the bangles on its lower end, NO paw; 6) viewer-left paw-hand ONLY (palm, fingers, claws, pads), a separate piece with a short wrist stub; 7) viewer-right furred forearm with bangles, NO paw; 8) viewer-right paw-hand ONLY (palm, fingers, claws, pads), a separate piece with a short wrist stub; 9) viewer-left furred thigh from hip to knee; 10) viewer-right furred thigh; 11) viewer-left furred shin from knee to ankle, NO foot; 12) viewer-right furred shin, NO foot; 13) viewer-left paw-foot ONLY (paw, clawed foot or hoof), a separate piece with a short ankle stub; 14) viewer-right paw-foot ONLY (paw, clawed foot or hoof), a separate piece with a short ankle stub; 15) the complete TAIL as ONE separate piece (see TAIL RULE). There is NO 16th piece: the neck is NOT a separate piece, it exists only as the stub on the head and the connector on the body.\n\nJOINT SEPARATION RULE (CRITICAL, FIXES THE FOREARM-AND-PAW-IN-ONE-PIECE ERROR): the forearm and the paw-hand are TWO DIFFERENT pieces, and the shin and the foot are TWO DIFFERENT pieces. They must NEVER be drawn as one connected shape. In total the sheet has SIX arm pieces (viewer-left upper arm, forearm, paw-hand and viewer-right upper arm, forearm, paw-hand), NEVER four, and SIX leg pieces (2 thighs, 2 shins, 2 paw-feet). Draw the sheet as if every piece had been cut out with scissors and laid down on its own. FOREARM: draw it as a complete, CLOSED shape with its own softly rounded bottom end and its own outline all the way round, the bangles sitting fully INSIDE its lower end; it contains NO paw, NO fingers, NO claws, NO pads and no part of a paw. Below it leave a clearly visible band of EMPTY transparent space (at least 5% of the image height, about as tall as the paw itself) with nothing in it: no wrist stub, no bangle, no fur strand. PAW-HAND: only below that empty band does the paw begin, as its own complete CLOSED shape with its own outline, starting with a short fur wrist stub that has a clean flat top cut, then the palm, fingers, claws and pads. The outline of the forearm must NOT continue into the paw: the background must show through between them. The same applies to the shin and the foot (empty band of at least 4% of the image height): the shin is a complete closed shape ending in a softly rounded ankle end, and the foot starts below the gap with its own short ankle stub that has a clean flat top cut. Paw-hands and paw-feet do NOT overlap, touch or merge with the forearm or shin, and the bangles never connect the forearm to the paw-hand. Paw-hand and paw-foot pieces are drawn at the same scale and in the same fur colour and markings as the forearm and shin, so they fit back onto them. ARM COLUMN CHECK: each arm column shows exactly THREE separate shapes (upper arm, forearm, paw-hand) and each leg column shows exactly THREE separate shapes (thigh, shin, foot). If a column shows only two shapes where there should be three (a forearm with the paw still attached is the most common mistake), redraw it with the paw cut off and moved down.\n\nNECK RULE (CRITICAL): the character has ONE neck, which on this sheet is split between exactly TWO pieces and nowhere else: the HEAD piece carries a short NECK STUB hanging straight down from the chin, and the BODY piece carries a short, THICK NECK CONNECTOR standing straight UP out of the blouse neckline. NEVER draw any loose, floating or free-standing neck piece, neck rectangle, neck capsule or neck cylinder anywhere on the sheet; every neck pixel belongs either to the head piece or to the body piece. The stub and the connector are IDENTICAL TWIN COLUMNS: exactly the same WIDTH (same pixel width), same THICKNESS, same length, same neck fur colour, markings and short fur texture, same shading and same outline weight, each a plain, sturdy, straight-sided vertical cylinder with perfectly PARALLEL, perfectly VERTICAL side edges: NO taper, NO flare, NO bell or funnel shape, NO widening toward the jaw or the shoulders, NO narrowing toward the neckline, NO collar-like or shoulder-like widening, NO curved or bulging edges, NO Adam's-apple bump. MASTER WIDTH: the HEAD STUB sets the master width and the BODY CONNECTOR is drawn EXACTLY AS THICK. The head stub is the natural width of the reference neck under the chin, about 45 to 50 percent of the head width at the cheeks, which is also about one quarter of the body's shoulder width (roughly 7 percent of the image width). Draw the head stub FIRST, then copy it exactly (same width, same thickness, same length, same outline) for the body connector, turned so its cut end points UP. BODY CONNECTOR THICKNESS (CRITICAL): the connector on the body piece is a THICK, sturdy column at the full stub width, measured at its top cut end AND at its base where it meets the neckline. It is NEVER a thin peg, stick, pin or pencil-like neck, NEVER thinner, narrower or slimmer than the head stub, and NEVER less than about 45 percent of the head width at the cheeks; the head stub is NEVER wider than the body connector: the two are exactly equal. Do NOT squeeze the connector to fit the neckline; instead cut the blouse neckline as a wide, soft, open scoop (at least 1.5 times as wide as the connector) so that the full-width, thick connector stands up out of it with both of its straight side edges clearly visible above the neckline. Each half is about as long as it is wide, or slightly shorter (roughly 3 to 4 percent of the image height), and perfectly vertical even if the head is turned. MATCHING NECK FUR (CRITICAL): the neck is NOT human skin and NOT a different material from the head. BOTH halves carry the neck fur of the reference (the plain fur of the neck itself directly under the chin: its colour, any stripes or markings and its short fur texture, NOT the chest ruff or bib below it), never taken from the forearms, sleeves or clothing, and the body connector is an exact copy of the head stub. The fur lies short and flat INSIDE a clean silhouette: both side edges stay plain straight parallel lines with NO fur tufts, NO ruff, NO mane and NO jagged or fuzzy edge sticking out; the longer cheek, chin, beard or mane fur belongs to the head, stops at the jaw and never hangs over the stub. ASSEMBLY TEST: laid together, the left and right edges of the stub continue as the left and right edges of the connector in two unbroken straight vertical lines, with no step, ledge, bulge, narrowing or widening; laid side by side the two halves measure the same width, and if the connector looks thinner than the stub, redraw it thicker, at the stub's full width. Each half ends in a clean FLAT cut (straight end, slightly rounded corners) with NO dark outline across the cut end; dark outlines run only along the exposed side edges. Each half contains ONLY neck: NO collar, NO shoulder, NO chest, NO clothing, NO necklace, NO hair pixels. If the pallu, dupatta or a high neckline covers the neck in the reference, STILL draw the connector standing up out of the neckline with the cloth folded down around its base. The top of the body around the connector is a clean smooth neckline: NO notch, NO cut-out, NO puzzle-shaped slot, NO hole where the connector sits. Leave a clear transparent gap of at least 3% of the image height between the cut end of the head stub and the cut end of the body connector so they never touch.\n\nHEAD STUB LOCK (CRITICAL, FIXES THE EXTRA SHOULDER-LIKE PART UNDER THE HEAD): the part of the head piece below the chin is ONLY a short neck stub, and it is an exact copy of the neck connector on the body piece: the same plain straight-sided column, the same width in pixels, the same length, the same fur colour, markings, texture, shading and outline weight, with a flat cut at its bottom end, hanging straight down from the middle of the chin. The head piece ENDS at that stub: below the chin there is NO shoulder, NO collarbone, NO trapezius slope, NO neckline, NO chest, NO chest fur, NO throat ruff, NO cream or white fur bib, NO sloping or flaring wedge, NO triangle, NO trapezoid and NO widening of any kind, and nothing on the head piece is wider than the connector. The white or cream chest or throat fur of the reference belongs to the BODY piece chest area only (below its connector); it never appears on the head piece below the chin line and it never changes the colour of the stub. Use ONE neck colour for both halves. SELF-CHECK before export: lay the head stub next to the body connector; they must look identical in width, length, colour and shape. If the part under the head is wider than the connector or has shoulder-like slopes, redraw it as a plain column.\n\nBODY PIECE RULE (CRITICAL): the body (piece 2) is the COMPLETE garment as ONE connected piece from top to bottom: the short, thick furred neck connector (as wide as the head's neck stub), BOTH SHOULDERS (plain rounded blouse-colour caps, see SHOULDER AND PALLU RULE), the blouse / choli / top with its neckline and any bare furred midriff shown in the reference, and the ENTIRE saree from where the cloth starts at the waist to its other end (the wrapped cloth across the chest, the pleats, the pallu or dupatta including any part that hangs down the shoulder, back or side) OR the entire frock / kameez / lehenga / gown skirt, all the way down to the hem. Necklaces and mangalsutra belong to this piece, on the chest below the neck connector. The body contains NO sleeves, NO sleeve pieces or sleeve fragments, NO sleeve borders or sleeve trim bands (no gold stripe and no floral band at its sides), NO upper arms, NO arm stubs, NO forearms, NO paw-hands (sleeve fabric and sleeve borders exist ONLY on pieces 3 and 4; furred arms and bangles ONLY on pieces 5 and 7). A pallu fall hanging beside the body is part of this same piece. NO trouser wedge, NO pelvis piece, NO legs, NO feet, NO shoes and NO tail pixels on it. It must NOT be split into blouse, skirt, petticoat or pallu pieces. It is by far the tallest piece on the sheet.\n\nSHOULDER AND PALLU RULE (CRITICAL): the body has proper SHOULDERS but NOTHING of the arms or sleeves. From the base of the neck connector the blouse shoulder lines slope gently outward and down on BOTH sides to the shoulder points, matching the reference shoulder width, and each ends in a short, softly rounded SHOULDER CAP exactly where the sleeve seam begins in the reference. Each shoulder cap is PLAIN blouse colour only (no stripe, no border, no print, no sleeve hem) and is the outermost part of the body at that height: no sleeve or arm shape sticks out sideways beyond it. The sleeve of the reference (the short sleeve with its gold and floral border band, including any sleeve hem or puff) is COMPLETELY REMOVED from the body and exists ONLY on the two upper-arm pieces 3 and 4. So at the two sides of the body there is NO sleeve, NO sleeve fragment, NO sleeve border, NO gold stripe, NO floral band, NO arm stub and NO upper-arm shape; below the shoulder caps the sides of the body are just the plain torso sides with clean rounded edges. The outer edge of each shoulder cap is a soft rounded cut with NO dark outline on that hidden joining edge, so that the rounded top of the upper-arm piece can overlap it cleanly. Both shoulders are symmetric in size and both are clearly visible. SAREE PALLU / ACHOL ON THE SHOULDER: the pallu (achol) that is draped over the character's shoulder (the viewer-right shoulder when the reference shows the pallu going over the character's left shoulder) must be drawn THIN and NARROW where it crosses the shoulder: a slim, neat band of folded cloth, only about one third to one half of the shoulder width, much thinner and less wide than a full draped pallu. It must NOT cover the whole shoulder. Because the pallu band is slim, the BLOUSE SHOULDER and the BLOUSE CORNER on that side (shoulder seam, shoulder point and armhole corner) stay clearly visible beside the pallu band, exactly as clearly as the blouse corner on the other side. The pallu fall that hangs down beside the body from this shoulder is also kept slim and compact, a modest vertical drape that stays within the body piece and does not widen the body silhouette more than the reference needs. Keep the border and floral print of the pallu, only make the shoulder part slimmer.\n\nSTRETCH-READY GARMENT RULE (CRITICAL): the lower garment will be stretched and squashed by the animation, so keep it simple. From the hips down draw long smooth VERTICAL pleats and folds that run straight down to the hem; prints and borders are simple repeating patterns. NO horizontal seams, tiers, flounces, belts or ribbons in the lower half; NO jewellery, tassels or small objects hanging on the skirt; NO strong cast shadows. The bottom edge is ONE clean continuous hem line (flat or very gently wavy) with the border band running along it. The hem ends at ankle height (saree, lehenga, long frock, gown) or where the reference shows it (knee or calf for a short frock or kurti), so the feet or lower legs stay visible below it. The skirt part is wide enough at the bottom to completely hide both legs, the tops of both paw-feet and the tail root. The garment is ONE smooth cloth shape: NO leg outlines, NO knee bumps, NO leg-separation line, NO slit showing legs.\n\nLEG PIECE RULE (CRITICAL): the six leg pieces (9-14) are drawn NORMALLY as plain, complete, bare furred legs in the FUR COLOUR (and stripes or markings) of the reference character's legs, exactly as they would look with the garment removed; if the reference hides the legs, use the fur colour and markings of the forearms. NO saree, NO petticoat, NO dress fabric, NO hem, NO border, NO pleats, NO garment colour and NO garment shadow on any leg piece. Keep the thighs and shins simple: perfectly straight, upright, slightly tapered capsules in the same fur colour as the forearms and paw-hands, with softly rounded ends and short flat fur inside a clean silhouette (no fur tufts sticking out); the two paw-feet are NOT upright capsules: they are right-facing side-profile paw-feet, see the FEET rule. Both thighs share one width and one length; both shins share one width and one length. The thigh, the shin and the foot are three SEPARATE shapes in each leg column (see JOINT SEPARATION RULE). FEET (RIGHT-FACING SIDE PROFILE, CRITICAL): everything else on this sheet keeps its front view, but BOTH PAW-FEET are drawn in a pure SIDE PROFILE turned to the VIEWER'S RIGHT. Each foot is ONE separate paw-foot, clawed foot or hoof piece: a short ankle stub with a clean flat top cut at the very top of the piece, standing on the heel half of the foot (the stub sits about one quarter of the foot length in from the heel end), and below it the paw lying horizontally, seen from the side, with the HEEL on the LEFT, the sole as one flat straight line along the bottom, the instep arching gently over the top, and the TOES, claws or hoof pointing to the RIGHT (toe pads and claw tips at the front tip, as the species has them). The viewer-left paw-foot and the viewer-right paw-foot BOTH face the SAME way, to the viewer's right: the second foot is an exact copy of the first, NEVER mirrored, so the two feet never face each other and never face away from each other. The toes and claws NEVER point down, NEVER point toward the viewer and NEVER point left. A side-profile paw-foot is wider than it is tall (about 2 to 2.5 times as long as it is high, not counting the stub): keep the ankle stub on the same vertical line as the shin above it, let the foot extend to the right of that line, and keep the toe tip at least 3% of the image width away from the body, the tail, the arm column and every other piece. The flat stub cut is the topmost part of the piece, and the stub is about 25 to 30 percent of the piece's total height. Feet are the only leg parts that stay visible, so draw them fully, in the same fur colour and markings as the shin: exactly as in the reference but turned to the right-facing side view (with the sandal, anklet or toe ring shown there, also drawn from the side); if the reference hides the feet or shows them from the front, draw natural paw-feet of that species in the same fur colour, still in right-facing side profile. LEG-WEAR EXCEPTION: if the reference shows other leg-wear below the hem (churidar, salwar, leggings, stockings, socks), draw the shins and feet in that leg-wear instead of bare fur; thighs stay furred.\n\nARM AND HAND RULE: each arm is THREE separate pieces (upper arm, forearm, paw-hand). Upper arms carry only the blouse sleeve and its trim, with a softly rounded top that overlaps the body's shoulder. The WHOLE sleeve of the reference, with its gold and floral border band, is drawn ONLY on the two upper-arm pieces, never on the body. Forearms are furred from elbow to wrist and end in a softly rounded wrist end; bangles, bracelets and watches belong to the forearm piece at its lower end and NEVER to the paw-hand piece. Paw-hands are separate paw-only pieces (palm, fingers, claws and pads in a relaxed natural pose hanging downward, fur or skin only as in the reference; nail polish, mehndi, rings or claws only if in the reference) with a short wrist stub and a clean flat top cut. Every paw-hand appears exactly once and is NEVER attached to a forearm (see JOINT SEPARATION RULE).\n\nTAIL RULE (CRITICAL): the tail (piece 15) is mandatory. Draw the COMPLETE tail as ONE single separate silhouette, root (the end that attaches to the lower back) at the TOP and tip at the BOTTOM, hanging straight down or in a gentle S-curve, with the exact fur colours, stripes, rings, tuft or scales of the reference and the full length including the tip. The root end is a clean softly rounded cut with NO garment pixels on it. If the tail is hidden in the reference, still draw the natural tail of that species in the same fur colours and pattern. The tail is never cut into several pieces and never attached to another piece.\n\nBEAST-GIRL RULES: the character has a HUMAN-LIKE UPRIGHT BODY (two arms, two legs, torso) with an ANIMAL HEAD. Wherever a human would have hair, skin, nose or ears, use the animal's own fur, scales, skin, muzzle, snout, ears, horns, mane and whiskers exactly as in the reference (the character's own hair, if any, stays as drawn there). The muzzle and nose are permanent head artwork; ears, horns and mane stay on the head piece and touch no other piece. Fur or scale texture and colours match the reference on every piece.\n\nBLANK EYE AREA RULE (CRITICAL, FIXES THE EYEBROWS AND THE EMPTY EYE SOCKETS): the head has NO eyes and NO eyebrows of any kind. The eye area is NOT an empty socket, NOT a hollow, NOT a blank white or cream oval or disc, NOT a pale patch, NOT an outlined shape and NOT a hole: it is simply the same FUR (the same face fur colour, markings, shading and texture as the cheeks and forehead around it) continuing smoothly and unbroken across the place where the eyes would be, exactly as if the character had never had eyes. There is NO eyeball, NO iris, NO pupil, NO highlight, NO eye outline, NO eyelid, NO eyelid crease, NO eyelash, NO lash line, NO socket shadow, NO white or cream eye patch and NO dark stroke or arc anywhere around the eye area. There are NO eyebrows: NO brow lines, NO brow strokes, NO brow marks, NO brow tufts, NO brow shadows, NO darker or lighter fur patch shaped like a brow and NO thin dark line above the eye area; fringe or bangs hair may hang on the forehead, but it stays clearly above the eye area and never reads as a brow. The face looks like a smooth, blank, eyeless face with only the muzzle, nose, mouth line, cheek fur, ears and hair; the eyes and eyebrows are added later as separate pieces.\n\nNO-TOUCH GAP AND SOLID PIECE RULE (CRITICAL): the sheet is read by counting separate connected silhouettes, so every one of the 15 pieces must be completely surrounded by transparent pixels, with a gap of at least 3% of the image height between neighbours in every column and around the body and around the whole tail (at least 5% between forearm and paw-hand, at least 4% between shin and foot). If two pieces touch anywhere, redraw them further apart. Every piece is fully opaque, solid artwork with a crisp clean outline; no faded, ghosted, semi-transparent, glowing or blurred areas, no soft alpha fade at any cut edge, no leftover background matte.\n\nANGLE, IDENTITY AND RIG RULES: keep the reference head at its exact natural front or three-quarter angle and keep every other piece at its original angle; do not turn the character into a side profile unless the reference is side-on. ONE EXCEPTION: the two FOOT pieces are ALWAYS drawn as right-facing side-profile paw-feet (see the FEET rule), even when the reference shows the feet from the front or hides them; the head, body, arms, paw-hands and tail keep their reference angles. The 15 parts must reassemble into the same character. Every joint end is softly rounded (shoulder, elbow, wrist, hip, knee, ankle) with no sharp tips and no separate oval or crescent joint pieces. At EVERY edge where one piece is designed to overlap another (shoulder to upper arm, upper arm to forearm, forearm to paw-hand, thigh top, thigh to shin, shin to foot) remove the dark outline from that hidden joining edge and continue the same colour to the edge; dark outlines only around exposed outer contours, including the whole outer contour and hem of the body.\n\nNEGATIVE PROMPT: sleeves on the body, sleeve stubs or sleeve fragments on the body, sleeve borders or sleeve hems on the body, gold stripe or floral band at the side of the body, short sleeve attached to the body, arm stubs count exactly 15 isolated pieces: head (with its own neck stub), ONE complete garment body (with its own neck connector and both plain shoulder caps), 2 upper arms, 2 forearms, 2 paw-hands, 2 thighs, 2 shins, 2 paw-feet and ONE tail, each separated from all others by visible transparent space. Check the four side columns one by one: the left arm column shows THREE shapes (upper arm, forearm, paw-hand), the right arm column shows THREE shapes, the left leg column shows THREE shapes (thigh, shin, foot), the right leg column shows THREE shapes. Every paw-hand is separate from its forearm and every foot is separate from its shin, with empty transparent space between them. Both paw-feet are right-facing side-profile feet: heel on the left, flat sole along the bottom, toes and claws pointing to the right, ankle stub on top at the heel end; both face the same direction, neither is mirrored, neither is seen from the front and none points down. The space between the head and the body is completely empty: no extra neck pieces. The head stub and the body connector are exactly identical in width, thickness, length and colour: the body connector is a THICK, sturdy furred column, NOT a thin peg, and the stub is NOT wider than the connector. The body runs unbroken from the neck connector and plain shoulder caps to the hem, is the tallest piece, and has NO sleeves, NO sleeve fragments, NO sleeve border or gold or floral trim at its sides, NO arm parts, NO legs and NO tail on it; the sleeve with its border exists only on the two upper-arm pieces. The pallu on the shoulder is slim and narrow, and the blouse shoulder corner is clearly visible on both sides. The six leg pieces are plain furred legs in fur colour with no garment fabric. The tail is one complete separate piece, root at the top, with no garment pixels on it. function twoSheetUi(id){ return document.getElementById(id); }
function twoSheetUiType(){ return (typeof activeBeastMan!=='undefined'&&activeBeastMan)?'beastman':(twoSheetUploadState.type||activeSheetType||'humanoid'); }
function twoSheetTypeName(type){ return type==='animal'?'Animal':type==='bird'?'Bird':type==='beastman'?'Beast Men':type==='mermaid'?'Mermaid':type==='lamia'?'Naga / Lamia':'Humanoid'; }
function updateTwoSheetLabels(){
  const type=twoSheetUploadState.type||activeSheetType||'humanoid', uiType=twoSheetUiType(), beast=uiType==='beastman', name=twoSheetTypeName(uiType), species=type==='animal'||type==='bird'||isMythicModelType(type), winged=isWingedHumanoidType(type);
  const title=twoSheetUi('autoRigUploadTitle');
  const baseTitle=document.querySelector('#baseSheetUploadArea')?.closest('.two-sheet-card')?.querySelector('.two-sheet-card-title');
  const variantTitle=document.querySelector('#variantSheetUploadArea')?.closest('.two-sheet-card')?.querySelector('.two-sheet-card-title');
  const create=twoSheetUi('twoSheetCreateBtn');
  if(title)title.textContent='Create a two-sheet '+name+' model';
  if(baseTitle)baseTitle.textContent=type==='bird'?'Base Body & Wings':type==='animal'?'Base Animal Body':beast?'Base Beast-Man Body':winged?'Base Body + 2 Back Wings':'Base Character Body';
  if(variantTitle)variantTitle.textContent=species?'Face, Mouth & Expression Variations':beast?'Muzzle, Face & Paw Variations':'Face & Gesture Variations';
  const bl=twoSheetUi('baseSheetUploadLabel'),bh=twoSheetUi('baseSheetUploadHint'),vl=twoSheetUi('variantSheetUploadLabel'),vh=twoSheetUi('variantSheetUploadHint');
  if(bl&&!twoSheetUploadState.base)bl.textContent='Upload '+(species?(type==='bird'?'7-part bird base sheet':isMythicModelType(type)?'11-part '+name.toLowerCase()+' base sheet':'15-part animal base sheet'):beast?'15-part beast-man base sheet':winged?'16-part '+name.toLowerCase()+' base sheet':'14-part base sheet');
  if(bh)bh.textContent='';
  if(vl&&!twoSheetUploadState.variants)vl.textContent=twoSheetUploadState.base?(type==='animal'?'Upload 11-part animal face-kit sheet (3 columns × 5 rows)':type==='bird'?'Upload 7-part bird face and flying-wing sheet':(beast?'Upload 19-part beast-man face and paw sheet':'Upload 19-part humanoid face and hand sheet')):'Upload the base sheet first';
  if(vh)vh.textContent='';
  if(create)create.textContent='Create rigged '+name.toLowerCase();
  document.querySelectorAll('.ev-sheet-copy-sq').forEach(b=>{
    const t='Copy '+uiType+(b.dataset.copy==='variant'?' expression prompt':' base-sheet prompt');
    b.title=t;b.setAttribute('aria-label',t);
  });
  document.querySelectorAll('#autoRigUploadBackdrop .ev-sheet-type-btn').forEach(btn=>{
    const active=btn.dataset.sheetType===uiType;btn.classList.toggle('active',active);btn.setAttribute('aria-pressed',active?'true':'false');
  });
}

function twoSheetResetPreview(prefix){
  const preview=twoSheetUi(prefix+'PreviewImg'), icon=twoSheetUi(prefix+'UploadIcon');
  const area=twoSheetUi(prefix+'UploadArea');
  if(preview){ if(preview.src && preview.src.startsWith('blob:')) URL.revokeObjectURL(preview.src); preview.removeAttribute('src'); preview.style.display='none'; }
  if(icon) icon.style.display='';
  if(area) area.classList.remove('ready');
}
function updateTwoSheetStatus(){
  const baseOK=!!twoSheetUploadState.base, variantOK=!!twoSheetUploadState.variants;
  const b=twoSheetUi('baseSheetStatus'), v=twoSheetUi('variantSheetStatus'), btn=twoSheetUi('twoSheetCreateBtn');
  if(b){b.textContent='';b.classList.toggle('ready',baseOK);}
  if(v){v.textContent='';v.classList.toggle('ready',variantOK);}
  if(btn)btn.disabled=!(baseOK&&variantOK);
  updateTwoSheetLabels();
}

  if(b)b.textContent='';if(v)v.textContent='';
};
function resetTwoSheetUploadUI(){
  const type=activeSheetType||'humanoid';
  twoSheetUploadState={base:null,variants:null,baseName:'Model',type};
  twoSheetResetPreview('baseSheet'); twoSheetResetPreview('variantSheet');
  const b=twoSheetUi('baseSheetStatus'),v=twoSheetUi('variantSheetStatus');if(b){b.textContent='';b.classList.remove('ready');}if(v){v.textContent='';v.classList.remove('ready');}
  updateTwoSheetLabels();updateTwoSheetStatus();
}
function loadImageFile(file, onLoaded, onError){
  const url=URL.createObjectURL(file), img=new Image();
  img.onload=()=>onLoaded(img,url);
  img.onerror=()=>{ URL.revokeObjectURL(url); if(onError) onError(); };
  img.src=url;
}
function handleBaseSheetFile(input){
  const file=input.files&&input.files[0]; input.value=''; if(!file) return;
  const type=twoSheetUploadState.type||activeSheetType||'humanoid', species=type==='animal'||type==='bird'||isMythicModelType(type), winged=isWingedHumanoidType(type);
  loadImageFile(file,(img,url)=>{
    let parsed; const beast=!species&&!!activeBeastMan;
    try{ parsed=sheetParse(img,type,beast,!species&&type==='humanoid'&&!!activeFemaleDress); }catch(err){ console.error(err); const kind=isMythicModelType(type)?twoSheetTypeName(type)+' 11-part':species?(type==='bird'?'7-part bird':'15-part animal'):(beast?'15-part beast-man':'14-part '+type); showToast('Could not analyze the '+kind+' base sheet',{icon:toastIcons.info,duration:2400}); URL.revokeObjectURL(url); return; }
    if(isMythicModelType(type) && parsed && !parsed.error && parsed.layout!=='mythic11'){
      showToast('Could not read the '+twoSheetTypeName(type)+' base sheet. It needs the head, body, two upper arms, two forearms, two hands and the tail, each separated by transparent space. Copy the new '+twoSheetTypeName(type)+' base-sheet prompt and make the sheet again.',{icon:toastIcons.info,duration:4800});
      URL.revokeObjectURL(url); return;
    }
    if(species && parsed && parsed.layout==='species14'){
      showToast('Found 14 pieces and no separate tail. Every '+type+' needs its own tail piece so it gets a tail bone. Copy the updated '+(type==='bird'?'7-part bird':'15-part animal')+' prompt and make the sheet again.',{icon:toastIcons.info,duration:4800});
      URL.revokeObjectURL(url); return;
    }
    if(beast && parsed && (parsed.layout==='anatomy14' || parsed.layout==='base14')){
      showToast('Found 14 pieces and no separate tail. A beast man now needs its own tail piece (15 parts) so it gets a tail bone. Copy the updated beast-man base-sheet prompt and make the sheet again.',{icon:toastIcons.info,duration:4800});
      URL.revokeObjectURL(url); return;
    }
    const expectedBaseKeys=parsed.layout==='mythic11'?SHEET_KEYS_MYTHIC_BASE:parsed.layout==='winged16'?SHEET_KEYS_WINGED_HUMANOID_16:parsed.layout==='bird7'?SHEET_KEYS_BIRD_7:parsed.layout==='bird13'?SHEET_KEYS_BIRD_13:(parsed.layout==='species15'?SHEET_KEYS_SPECIES_15:(parsed.layout==='beast15'?SHEET_KEYS_BEAST_15:SHEET_KEYS_14));
    if(parsed.error || !parsed.arts || !expectedBaseKeys.every(k=>parsed.arts[k])){
      showToast(parsed.error||(isMythicModelType(type)?'The '+twoSheetTypeName(type)+' base sheet must contain the head, body, two upper arms, two forearms, two hands and the tail as separate isolated pieces.':winged?'The '+twoSheetTypeName(type)+' base sheet must contain exactly 16 parts: the 14-part humanoid body and two separate complete back wings.':species?(type==='bird'?'The new bird base sheet must contain exactly 7 parts: blank head, body, tail, two complete default wings and two complete legs with feet.':'The animal base sheet must contain exactly 15 separate pieces, including one independent tail.'):(beast?'The beast-man base sheet must contain exactly 15 separate pieces, including one independent tail.':'The base sheet must contain exactly 14 separate anatomy pieces.')),{icon:toastIcons.info,duration:3000});
      URL.revokeObjectURL(url); return;
    }
    twoSheetUploadState.base=parsed.arts;twoSheetUploadState.variants=null;
    twoSheetUploadState.baseName=(file.name||'Model').replace(/\.[^.]+$/,'').slice(0,24)||'Model';
    if(!species){
      if(twoSheetUploadState.base.torso)twoSheetUploadState.base.torso.bodyIncludesNeckAndWaist=true;
      if(twoSheetUploadState.base.head)twoSheetUploadState.base.head.recoveredHeadWithoutNeck=true;
    }
    twoSheetUploadState.dress=!!parsed.dress;
    if(parsed.dress)edApplyDressPivot(twoSheetUploadState.base);
    const preview=twoSheetUi('baseSheetPreviewImg'),icon=twoSheetUi('baseSheetUploadIcon'),area=twoSheetUi('baseSheetUploadArea');
    if(preview){preview.src=url;preview.style.display='block';}if(icon)icon.style.display='none';if(area)area.classList.add('ready');
    const label=twoSheetUi('baseSheetUploadLabel'),hint=twoSheetUi('baseSheetUploadHint');
    if(label)label.textContent='Base sheet ready';if(hint)hint.textContent='';
    twoSheetResetPreview('variantSheet');
    updateTwoSheetStatus();
    showToast(Object.keys(parsed.arts).length+'-part '+(beast?'beast-man':twoSheetTypeName(type))+' base sheet accepted. Now upload the matching face-expression sheet.',{duration:2300});
  },()=>showToast('Could not open that base-body image',{icon:toastIcons.info,duration:1800}));
}
// Animal face kit: counts transparent pixels fully ENCLOSED inside a head piece (the hollow mouth) - transparent
// pixels that cannot be reached from the piece border through other transparent pixels.
function animalHeadHollowPixels(art){
  if(!art || !art.alpha || !(art.w>0) || !(art.h>0)) return 0;
  const w=art.w, h=art.h, a=art.alpha, seen=new Uint8Array(w*h), stack=[];
  const push=(x,y)=>{ const i=y*w+x; if(!a[i] && !seen[i]){ seen[i]=1; stack.push(i); } };
  for(let x=0;x<w;x++){ push(x,0); push(x,h-1); }
  for(let y=0;y<h;y++){ push(0,y); push(w-1,y); }
  while(stack.length){
    const i=stack.pop(), x=i%w, y=(i-x)/w;
    if(x>0)push(x-1,y); if(x<w-1)push(x+1,y); if(y>0)push(x,y-1); if(y<h-1)push(x,y+1);
  }
  let n=0; for(let i=0;i<w*h;i++) if(!a[i] && !seen[i]) n++;
  return n;
}
// Names of the emotion heads that do NOT have a hollow mouth (empty list = all four heads are hollow).
function animalFaceKitHollowReport(arts){
  const names={head_sad:'Sad',head_angry:'Angry',head_shock:'Surprised',head_laugh:'Happy'}, missing=[];
  Object.keys(names).forEach(k=>{
    const art=arts&&arts[k]; if(!art) return;
    const need=Math.max(60,Math.round(art.w*art.h*0.0006));
    if(animalHeadHollowPixels(art)<need) missing.push(names[k]);
  });
  return missing;
}
function parseVariantSheetImage(img,type){
  if(type==='animal'){
    const kit=sheetParse(img,'animal-variants');
    if(!kit.error&&kit.layout==='animalVariants11'&&SHEET_KEYS_ANIMAL_VARIANTS.every(k=>kit.arts&&kit.arts[k]))return {arts:kit.arts,layout:'animalVariants11'};
    return {error:kit.error||'The animal face-kit sheet must contain exactly 11 isolated pieces in the locked 3-column by 5-row layout.'};
  }
  if(type==='bird'){
    const compactBird=sheetParse(img,'bird-variants');
    if(!compactBird.error&&compactBird.layout==='birdVariants7'&&SHEET_KEYS_BIRD_VARIANTS_7.every(k=>compactBird.arts&&compactBird.arts[k]))return {arts:compactBird.arts,layout:'birdVariants7'};
    if(!compactBird.error&&compactBird.layout==='birdVariants6'&&SHEET_KEYS_BIRD_VARIANTS_6.every(k=>compactBird.arts&&compactBird.arts[k]))return {arts:compactBird.arts,layout:'birdVariants6'};
    const legacyBird=sheetParse(img,'species-variants');
    if(!legacyBird.error&&legacyBird.layout==='speciesVariants9'&&SHEET_KEYS_SPECIES_VARIANTS.every(k=>legacyBird.arts&&legacyBird.arts[k]))return {arts:legacyBird.arts,layout:'speciesVariants9'};
    return {error:compactBird.error||legacyBird.error||'The new bird face and flying-wing sheet must contain exactly seven parts in a 3×3 grid. Existing six-part and legacy nine-part sheets are also supported.'};
  }
  if(type==='animal'){
    const species=sheetParse(img,'species-variants');
    if(!species.error&&species.layout==='speciesVariants9'&&SHEET_KEYS_SPECIES_VARIANTS.every(k=>species.arts&&species.arts[k]))return {arts:species.arts,layout:'speciesVariants9'};
    return {error:species.error||'The animal expression sheet must contain exactly 9 isolated face and mouth pieces in a 3×3 layout.'};
  }
  const compact=sheetParse(img,'humanoid-variants');
  if(!compact.error && (compact.layout==='humanoidVariants19' || compact.layout==='humanoidVariants17' || compact.layout==='variants16' || compact.layout==='variants13')) return {arts:compact.arts,layout:compact.layout};
  const legacy=sheetParse(img,'humanoid');
  if(!legacy.error && legacy.layout==='legacy27'){
    const extras={};Object.keys(legacy.arts||{}).forEach(k=>{if(!SHEET_KEYS_12.includes(k)&&!SHEET_KEYS_14.includes(k)&&legacy.arts[k])extras[k]=legacy.arts[k];});
    if(Object.keys(extras).length>=13)return {arts:extras,layout:'legacy27-variants'};
  }
  return {error:compact.error||legacy.error||'The new humanoid second sheet must contain 19 face and hand parts (incl. two closed eyes). Legacy 17/16/13-part and complete 27-part sheets are still supported.'};
}
function handleVariantSheetFile(input){
  const file=input.files&&input.files[0];input.value='';if(!file)return;
  const type=twoSheetUploadState.type||activeSheetType||'humanoid',species=type!=='humanoid';
  if(!twoSheetUploadState.base){showToast('Upload the base body sheet first',{icon:toastIcons.info,duration:2000});return;}
  loadImageFile(file,(img,url)=>{
    let parsed;try{parsed=parseVariantSheetImage(img,type);}catch(err){console.error(err);showToast('Could not analyze the expression sheet',{icon:toastIcons.info,duration:2400});URL.revokeObjectURL(url);return;}
    const keys=type==='animal'?SHEET_KEYS_ANIMAL_VARIANTS:type==='bird'?(parsed.layout==='birdVariants7'?SHEET_KEYS_BIRD_VARIANTS_7:parsed.layout==='birdVariants6'?SHEET_KEYS_BIRD_VARIANTS_6:SHEET_KEYS_SPECIES_VARIANTS):(parsed.layout==='variants13'?SHEET_KEYS_VARIANTS_13:parsed.layout==='humanoidVariants19'?SHEET_KEYS_HUMANOID_VARIANTS_19:parsed.layout==='humanoidVariants17'?SHEET_KEYS_HUMANOID_VARIANTS_17:SHEET_KEYS_VARIANTS_16);
    const hasFaceParts=!!parsed.arts&&Object.keys(parsed.arts).some(k=>['mouth_closed','mouth_open','mouth_hold','mouth_inner','eye_L','eye_R','eye_closed_L','eye_closed_R','head_blink','head_sad','head_angry','head_shock','head_laugh','bird_eye','bird_eye_closed','bird_brow','beak_closed','beak_open','wingL_fly','wingR_fly'].includes(k));
    const missingCompactPart=(parsed.layout==='animalVariants11'||parsed.layout==='speciesVariants9'||parsed.layout==='birdVariants7'||parsed.layout==='birdVariants6'||parsed.layout==='humanoidVariants19'||parsed.layout==='humanoidVariants17'||parsed.layout==='variants16'||parsed.layout==='variants13')&&(!parsed.arts||!keys.every(k=>parsed.arts[k]));
    if(parsed.error||!parsed.arts||!hasFaceParts||missingCompactPart){
      showToast(parsed.error||(type==='animal'?'The animal face-kit must contain all 11 locked parts.':type==='bird'?(parsed.layout==='birdVariants6'?'The legacy bird face/flight sheet must contain all 6 parts.':'The new bird face/flight sheet must contain all 7 parts.'):'The humanoid second sheet must contain all 19 face and hand pieces (including the two closed eyes).'),{icon:toastIcons.info,duration:3000});URL.revokeObjectURL(url);return;
    }
    twoSheetUploadState.variants=parsed.arts;
    const preview=twoSheetUi('variantSheetPreviewImg'),icon=twoSheetUi('variantSheetUploadIcon'),area=twoSheetUi('variantSheetUploadArea');
    if(preview){preview.src=url;preview.style.display='block';}if(icon)icon.style.display='none';if(area)area.classList.add('ready');
    const label=twoSheetUi('variantSheetUploadLabel'),hint=twoSheetUi('variantSheetUploadHint');
    if(label)label.textContent='Expression sheet ready';if(hint)hint.textContent='';
    updateTwoSheetStatus();
    let hollowMissing=[];
    if(type==='animal'){ try{ hollowMissing=animalFaceKitHollowReport(parsed.arts); }catch(_e){ hollowMissing=[]; } }
    if(hollowMissing.length) showToast('Face-kit accepted, but no hollow mouth was found in the '+hollowMissing.join(', ')+' head'+(hollowMissing.length>1?'s':'')+'. The mouth interior must be transparent (no tongue, throat or fill). Copy the updated face-kit prompt and regenerate.',{icon:toastIcons.info,duration:5600});
    else showToast(type==='animal'?'Animal face-expression sheet accepted.':type==='bird'?'Bird face and flight sheet accepted.':isMythicModelType(type)?twoSheetTypeName(type)+' face and gesture sheet accepted.':activeBeastMan?'Beast-man face and paw sheet accepted.':'Humanoid face and gesture sheet accepted.',{duration:1600});
  },()=>showToast('Could not open that expression sheet',{icon:toastIcons.info,duration:1800}));
}
function createFromTwoSheets(){
  if(!twoSheetUploadState.base||!twoSheetUploadState.variants){showToast('Upload both model sheets first',{icon:toastIcons.info,duration:1800});return;}
  const type=twoSheetUploadState.type||activeSheetType||'humanoid',species=type==='animal'||type==='bird'||isMythicModelType(type),mythic=isMythicModelType(type),winged=isWingedHumanoidType(type);
  const arts={...twoSheetUploadState.base,...twoSheetUploadState.variants};
  if(!species){if(arts.torso)arts.torso.bodyIncludesNeckAndWaist=true;if(arts.head)arts.head.recoveredHeadWithoutNeck=true;}
  const baseKeys=mythic?SHEET_KEYS_MYTHIC_BASE:(winged?SHEET_KEYS_WINGED_HUMANOID_16:(type==='bird'&&SHEET_KEYS_BIRD_7.every(k=>arts[k])?SHEET_KEYS_BIRD_7:(species&&type==='bird'&&SHEET_KEYS_BIRD_13.every(k=>arts[k])&&!arts.shoeL&&!arts.shoeR?SHEET_KEYS_BIRD_13:(species&&SHEET_KEYS_SPECIES_15.every(k=>arts[k])?SHEET_KEYS_SPECIES_15:(!species&&activeBeastMan&&arts.tail?SHEET_KEYS_BEAST_15:SHEET_KEYS_14)))));
  const missing=baseKeys.filter(k=>!arts[k]);
  if(missing.length){showToast('The base sheet is missing one or more required anatomy pieces. Please re-upload it.',{icon:toastIcons.info,duration:2600});return;}
  const name=twoSheetUploadState.baseName||twoSheetTypeName(type),kind=sheetCreateMode==='newAsset'?'newAsset':'new';
  const view=species&&type!=='mermaid'&&type!=='lamia'&&type!=='ghost'?'side':'front';
  let orderBase=SHEET_DEFAULT_ORDER;
  if(mythic)orderBase=SHEET_DEFAULT_ORDER_MYTHIC;
  else if(winged)orderBase=SHEET_DEFAULT_ORDER_WINGED;
  else if(type==='bird'&&baseKeys===SHEET_KEYS_BIRD_7)orderBase=SHEET_DEFAULT_ORDER_BIRD_7;
  else if(type==='humanoid'&&SHEET_KEYS_HUMANOID_VARIANTS_17.every(k=>arts[k]))orderBase=SHEET_DEFAULT_ORDER_HUMANOID_FACEKIT;
  else if(species)orderBase=SHEET_DEFAULT_ORDER_SIDE;
  const order=orderBase.filter(k=>arts[k]);
  const beast=!species&&!winged&&!!activeBeastMan;
  closeAutoRigUploader();
  const dress=!species&&!winged&&type==='humanoid'&&!!twoSheetUploadState.dress;
  if(dress&&arts.torso)arts.torso.dress=true;
  edOpen({kind,view,modelType:type,beast,dress,arts,st:edDefaultLayout(arts,view,type,{beast,dress}),order,name});
}

document.getElementById('baseSheetFileInput').addEventListener('change',e=>handleBaseSheetFile(e.target));
document.getElementById('variantSheetFileInput').addEventListener('change',e=>handleVariantSheetFile(e.target));

let activeSheetType = 'humanoid';
let activeBeastMan = false;
function copySpeciesPrompt(type){
  const t=type==='bird'?'bird':'animal';
  copyTextPrompt(SPECIES_PROMPTS[t],t==='bird'?'Bird 7-part base-sheet prompt':'Animal 15-part base-sheet prompt');
}
function copySpeciesVariantPrompt(type){
  const t=type==='bird'?'bird':'animal';
  copyTextPrompt(SPECIES_VARIANT_PROMPTS[t],t==='bird'?'Bird 7-part face and flying-wing prompt':'Animal 11-part face-kit prompt');
}
function evTypePromptEntry(){ return EV_TYPE_PROMPTS[activeBeastMan?'beastman':activeSheetType]||null; }
function copyActiveBaseSheetPrompt(){
  if(activeSheetType==='animal'||activeSheetType==='bird')return copySpeciesPrompt(activeSheetType);
  if(activeFemaleDress&&activeSheetType==='humanoid'){const f=EV_FEMALE_PROMPTS[activeBeastMan?'beastman':'humanoid'];return copyTextPrompt(f.base,f.baseLabel);}
  const e=evTypePromptEntry();
  if(e)return copyTextPrompt(e.base,e.baseLabel);
  return copyBody14Prompt();
}
function copyActiveVariantSheetPrompt(){
  if(activeSheetType==='animal'||activeSheetType==='bird')return copySpeciesVariantPrompt(activeSheetType);
  if(activeFemaleDress&&activeSheetType==='humanoid'){const f=EV_FEMALE_PROMPTS[activeBeastMan?'beastman':'humanoid'];return copyTextPrompt(f.variant,f.variantLabel);}
  const e=evTypePromptEntry();
  if(e)return copyTextPrompt(e.variant,e.variantLabel);
  return copyVariants16Prompt();
}
function copyActiveSheetPrompt(){return copyActiveBaseSheetPrompt();}
function evCopySheetPrompt(btn,which){
  /* base -> species 15-part / humanoid 14-part body prompt, variant -> face/expression prompt, for the selected model type */
  if(which==='variant')copyActiveVariantSheetPrompt();else copyActiveBaseSheetPrompt();
  if(!btn)return;
  btn.classList.add('copied');
  clearTimeout(btn._evCopyT);
  btn._evCopyT=setTimeout(function(){btn.classList.remove('copied');},1400);
}
function setUploadSheetType(type){
  const femaleDress=(type==='humanoidf'||type==='beastmanf');
  if(type==='humanoidf')type='humanoid';else if(type==='beastmanf')type='beastman';
  activeBeastMan=(type==='beastman');
  activeFemaleDress=femaleDress;
  activeSheetType=['animal','bird','mermaid','lamia'].includes(type)?type:'humanoid'; // v349: Devil, Fairy and Ghost are not offered in model upload.
  if(typeof twoSheetUploadState==='object'&&twoSheetUploadState){
    twoSheetUploadState.type=activeSheetType;
    if(typeof resetTwoSheetUploadUI==='function')resetTwoSheetUploadUI();
    else if(typeof updateTwoSheetLabels==='function')updateTwoSheetLabels();
  }
  if(typeof autoRigState!=='undefined'&&autoRigState)autoRigState.modelType=activeSheetType;
  const title=document.getElementById('autoRigUploadTitle'),sub=document.getElementById('sheetUploadSub'),copy=document.getElementById('sheetCopyExtBtn'),label=document.getElementById('autoRigUploadLabel'),hint=document.getElementById('autoRigUploadHint');
  if(title)title.textContent='Create a two-sheet '+twoSheetTypeName(twoSheetUiType())+' model';
  if(sub)sub.textContent='';
  if(copy)copy.textContent='Copy legacy 27-part humanoid prompt';
  if(label&&label.textContent!=='Analyzing…')label.textContent='Legacy combined sheet upload';
  if(hint)hint.textContent='';
  document.querySelectorAll('#autoRigUploadBackdrop .ev-sheet-type-btn').forEach(btn=>{const active=btn.dataset.sheetType===twoSheetUiType();btn.classList.toggle('active',active);btn.setAttribute('aria-pressed',active?'true':'false');});
  if(typeof updateTwoSheetLabels==='function')updateTwoSheetLabels();
}

function copyTextPrompt(text, label){
  const done = ()=>showToast(label+' copied. Paste it into your image AI', {icon:toastIcons.check, duration:2200});
  function fb(){ const t = document.createElement('textarea'); t.value = text; document.body.appendChild(t); t.select(); try{ document.execCommand('copy'); done(); }catch(e){ showToast('Copy failed'); } t.remove(); }
  if(navigator.clipboard && navigator.clipboard.writeText) navigator.clipboard.writeText(text).then(done, fb); else fb();
}
function copySheetPrompt(){ copyTextPrompt(SHEET_PROMPT_TURNED_EXT, '27-part humanoid model-sheet prompt'); }
function copySheetPromptExt(){ copySheetPrompt(); }
function copyBody14Prompt(){ copyTextPrompt(SHEET_PROMPT_TWO_BODY,'14-part base-body sheet prompt'); }
function copyVariants16Prompt(){ copyTextPrompt(SHEET_PROMPT_VARIANTS_16_NECK,'19-part expressions, closed eyes and gestures prompt'); }
function copyVariants13Prompt(){ return copyVariants16Prompt(); }

/* ---------- 1) parse the sheet ---------- */
document.getElementById('sheetFileInput').addEventListener('change', e=>{
  const f = e.target.files[0]; e.target.value = ''; if(!f) return;
  const url = URL.createObjectURL(f);
  document.getElementById('autoRigUploadPreviewImg').src = url;
  document.getElementById('autoRigUploadPreviewImg').style.display = 'block';
  document.getElementById('autoRigUploadIcon').style.display = 'none';
  document.getElementById('autoRigUploadLabel').textContent = 'Analyzing…';
  const resetTile = ()=>{
    document.getElementById('autoRigUploadPreviewImg').style.display = 'none';
    document.getElementById('autoRigUploadIcon').style.display = '';
    setUploadSheetType(activeSheetType);
  };
  const img = new Image();
  img.onload = ()=>{ try{ buildFromSheet(img, f.name, activeSheetType); }catch(err){ console.error(err); showToast("Couldn't read this sheet", {icon:toastIcons.info, duration:2400}); resetTile(); } };
  img.onerror = ()=>{ showToast("Couldn't open that image", {icon:toastIcons.info}); resetTile(); };
  img.src = url;
});
// Automatically detects dark outline strokes on hidden species attachment edges and blends
// them into the local coat/feather colour before a cut-out part is stored or sent to the rig editor.
// Only known mating zones are inspected; all exposed outer contours and internal markings remain intact.
function edCleanSpeciesJointEdge(canvas, key, modelType){
  if(modelType==='animal' && /^(?:head(?:_(?:sad|angry|shock|laugh))?|mouth_(?:closed|open|hold|inner)|eye_[LR]|eye_closed_[LR]|brow_[LR])$/.test(key)) return {__evJointCleanup:true, cleanedPixels:0};
  if(/^(?:mouth_inner|eye_[LR]|eye_closed_[LR]|brow_[LR])$/.test(key)) return {__evJointCleanup:true, cleanedPixels:0};
  if(!canvas || !canvas.width || !canvas.height) return {__evJointCleanup:true, cleanedPixels:0};
  const w=canvas.width,h=canvas.height,ctx=canvas.getContext('2d',{willReadFrequently:true});
  let image; try{image=ctx.getImageData(0,0,w,h);}catch(_e){return {__evJointCleanup:true, cleanedPixels:0};}
  const d=image.data,src=new Uint8ClampedArray(d); let minX=w,minY=h,maxX=-1,maxY=-1;
  for(let y=0;y<h;y++)for(let x=0;x<w;x++)if(src[(y*w+x)*4+3]>35){minX=Math.min(minX,x);maxX=Math.max(maxX,x);minY=Math.min(minY,y);maxY=Math.max(maxY,y);}
  if(maxX<minX||maxY<minY)return {__evJointCleanup:true,cleanedPixels:0};
  const bw=maxX-minX+1,bh=maxY-minY+1,short=Math.max(1,Math.min(bw,bh));
  const depth=Math.max(3,Math.min(13,Math.round(short*.075)));
  const zones=[];
  const add=(side,a,b,th=depth)=>zones.push({side,a,b,th});
  const animal=modelType==='animal', bird=modelType==='bird';
  const isHead=/^head(?:_|$)/.test(key), isMouth=/^mouth_(?:closed|open|hold)$/.test(key);
  if(isMouth){
    // The three talking overlays carry the nose/beak base too: erase only their outer patch
    // contour, never the dark mouth opening/lip detail in the middle.
    add('top',.02,.98); add('bottom',.02,.98); add('left',.02,.98); add('right',.02,.98);
  }else if(isHead){
    // Full head/face swaps attach along the lower jaw/neck cut.
    add('bottom',animal?.28:.25,animal?.82:.78);
  }else if(key==='torso'){
    if(animal){
      // Rump/tail root on the left, head/body connection at the right, and the two
      // underside leg-root windows. The visible belly contour between roots is preserved.
      add('left',.25,.76); add('right',.24,.76);
      add('bottom',.12,.39); add('bottom',.60,.88);
    }else{
      // Bird body: tail attachment at rear, head/neck at front, wing roots on the flanks,
      // and leg roots underneath. Restrict each zone to its expected anatomical window.
      add('left',.28,.72); add('right',.22,.55);
      add('top',.36,.68); add('bottom',.30,.70);
    }
  }else if(key==='tail'){
    add('right',.20,.80);
  }else if(/^arm[LR]_upper$/.test(key) || /^leg[LR]_upper$/.test(key)){
    // Proximal limb pieces connect to the body at the top and to the next segment at bottom.
    add('top',.02,.98); add('bottom',.02,.98);
  }else if(/^arm[LR]_lower$/.test(key)){
    add('top',.02,.98); add('bottom',.02,.98);
  }else if(/^leg[LR]_lower$/.test(key)){
    // Mammal lower legs have a separate paw at the ankle; bird lower legs include the foot,
    // so their bottom contour is exposed and must stay outlined.
    add('top',.02,.98);
    if(animal)add('bottom',.02,.98);
  }else if(/^(?:hand[LR]|shoe[LR])(?:_|$)/.test(key)){
    // The wrist/ankle opening is the top edge of a separate paw or foot piece.
    add('top',.10,.90);
  }
  if(!zones.length)return {__evJointCleanup:true,cleanedPixels:0};
  const lum=i=>.2126*src[i]+.7152*src[i+1]+.0722*src[i+2];
  const inside=(x,y,z)=>{
    let dd=0,t=0;
    if(z.side==='top'){dd=y-minY;t=(x-minX)/Math.max(1,bw-1);}
    else if(z.side==='bottom'){dd=maxY-y;t=(x-minX)/Math.max(1,bw-1);}
    else if(z.side==='left'){dd=x-minX;t=(y-minY)/Math.max(1,bh-1);}
    else {dd=maxX-x;t=(y-minY)/Math.max(1,bh-1);}
    return dd>=0&&dd<z.th&&t>=z.a&&t<=z.b;
  };
  function inwardSample(x,y,side,fromDepth){
    const vals=[];
    for(let along=-2;along<=2;along++){
      let xx=x,yy=y;
      if(side==='top'){xx+=along;yy=minY+fromDepth;}
      else if(side==='bottom'){xx+=along;yy=maxY-fromDepth;}
      else if(side==='left'){xx=minX+fromDepth;yy+=along;}
      else {xx=maxX-fromDepth;yy+=along;}
      if(xx<minX||xx>maxX||yy<minY||yy>maxY)continue;
      const i=(yy*w+xx)*4;if(src[i+3]<100)continue;
      const l=lum(i);if(l>45)vals.push([src[i],src[i+1],src[i+2],l]);
    }
    if(vals.length<2)return null;
    vals.sort((a,b)=>a[3]-b[3]);
    const bright=vals.filter(v=>v[3]>vals[0][3]+16);
    const chosen=bright.length>=2?bright:vals.slice(Math.floor(vals.length/2));
    if(!chosen.length)return null;
    const med=k=>{const a=chosen.map(v=>v[k]).sort((a,b)=>a-b);return a[Math.floor(a.length/2)];};
    return [med(0),med(1),med(2)];
  }
  let changed=0;
  for(let y=minY;y<=maxY;y++)for(let x=minX;x<=maxX;x++){
    const i=(y*w+x)*4,a=src[i+3];if(a<75)continue;
    let zone=null;
    for(let z=0;z<zones.length;z++)if(inside(x,y,zones[z])){zone=zones[z];break;}
    if(!zone)continue;
    const l=lum(i);if(l>188)continue;
    const inward=inwardSample(x,y,zone.side,Math.min(Math.max(3,zone.th-1),Math.max(3,Math.round(zone.th*.62))));
    if(!inward)continue;
    const il=.2126*inward[0]+.7152*inward[1]+.0722*inward[2];
    // Require a clear local outline-to-fill contrast. Dark fur patches with matching dark
    // colour immediately inward are deliberately left untouched.
    if(il-l<24 || l>il*.86)continue;
    for(let c=0;c<3;c++)d[i+c]=Math.round(src[i+c]*.06+inward[c]*.94);
    changed++;
  }
  ctx.putImageData(image,0,0);
  return {__evJointCleanup:true,cleanedPixels:changed};
}

// Remove dark contour/shadow pixels only on the small end zones that are meant to be hidden at a joint.
// The outer silhouette elsewhere (faces, hair, clothes and shoe soles) is untouched. We recolour contour
// pixels instead of erasing alpha, so antialiased edges stay closed when parts overlap.
function edCleanJointEdge(canvas, key, modelType){
  // v318: automatic joint-border removal is switched OFF for now. Parts keep their original artwork edges.
  // Set window.ETHVELLE_JOINT_BORDER_CLEANUP = true to bring the old behaviour back.
  if(!window.ETHVELLE_JOINT_BORDER_CLEANUP) return;
  if(!canvas || !canvas.width || !canvas.height) return;
  // Species art has its own anatomy-aware attachment zones. Run the automatic local
  // outline detector now, while each extracted piece is still a separate canvas and before
  // the model can be assembled/created in the rig editor.
  if(modelType === 'animal' || modelType === 'bird') return edCleanSpeciesJointEdge(canvas,key,modelType);
  const w=canvas.width, h=canvas.height, ctx=canvas.getContext('2d',{willReadFrequently:true});
  let im; try{ im=ctx.getImageData(0,0,w,h); }catch(e){ return; }
  const d=im.data, original=new Uint8ClampedArray(d), isHead=/^head(?:_|$)/.test(key), torso=key==='torso';
  let cleanedPixels=0;
  const isMouth=key==='mouth_closed'||key==='mouth_open'||key==='mouth_hold';
  // Mouth pieces are overlays on top of the full head. Remove dark pixels only within a few pixels
  // of the outer alpha silhouette, recolouring them to the same skin palette; internal lip/smile
  // details remain untouched because they are surrounded by opaque mouth/skin pixels.
  if(isMouth){
    const skinSamples=[[],[],[]];
    for(let p=0;p<w*h;p++){
      const i=p*4, a=original[i+3]; if(a<150) continue;
      const r=original[i],g=original[i+1],b=original[i+2];
      if(r>165 && g>75 && b>30 && r>g*1.12 && g>b*1.12 && r-g>28 && g-b>12){
        skinSamples[0].push(r); skinSamples[1].push(g); skinSamples[2].push(b);
      }
    }
    const med=a=>{ if(!a.length) return null; a.sort((x,y)=>x-y); return a[Math.floor(a.length/2)]; };
    const skin=skinSamples.map(med);
    if(skin.every(v=>v!==null)){
      for(let y=0;y<h;y++) for(let x=0;x<w;x++){
        const i=(y*w+x)*4; if(original[i+3]<35) continue;
        const lum=.2126*original[i]+.7152*original[i+1]+.0722*original[i+2];
        if(lum>220) continue;
        let nearOutside=false;
        for(let oy=-5;oy<=5&&!nearOutside;oy++) for(let ox=-5;ox<=5;ox++){
          if(ox*ox+oy*oy>64) continue;
          const nx=x+ox, ny=y+oy;
          if(nx<0||ny<0||nx>=w||ny>=h||original[(ny*w+nx)*4+3]<28){ nearOutside=true; break; }
        }
        if(!nearOutside) continue;
        // Preserve edge alpha for anti-aliasing while removing its dark RGB fringe.
        const blend=1;
        for(let c=0;c<3;c++) d[i+c]=Math.round(original[i+c]*(1-blend)+skin[c]*blend);
        cleanedPixels++;
      }
    }
    ctx.putImageData(im,0,0);
    return null;
  }
  const point=/^hand[LR]_point$/.test(key), thumbOrPalm=/^hand[LR]_(?:thumb|palm)$/.test(key);
  const hand=/^hand[LR](?:_fist)?$/.test(key), upperArm=/^arm[LR]_upper$/.test(key), lowerArm=/^arm[LR]_lower$/.test(key);
  const upperLeg=/^leg[LR]_upper$/.test(key), lowerLeg=/^leg[LR]_lower$/.test(key);
  const inJointBand=(x,y)=>{
    // Keep the jaw/chin silhouette intact. A recovered head ends at the face/jaw boundary,
    // not around the neck, so only the final few pixels are a hidden overlap zone.
    if(isHead) return y>=h*.97 && x>=w*.30 && x<=w*.70;
    if(torso){
      // The bottom of this Body image may contain the REAL dark-blue trouser/waist piece from the
      // uploaded sheet. It is artwork, not a hidden joint seam: never recolour/flatten the bottom
      // centre band, or the denim triangle can turn shirt-blue and look like an added blue patch.
      // Only clean the tiny shoulder/arm overlap corners; preserve the complete lower hem and
      // every pants-start pixel with its original RGB and alpha values.
      if(y>=h*.05 && y<=h*.27 && ((x<=w*.14)||(x>=w*.86))) return true;
      return false;
    }
    if(point){ return (key==='handL_point' ? x<=w*.10 : x>=w*.90) && y>=h*.22 && y<=h*.78; }
    if(thumbOrPalm) return y>=h*.89 && x>=w*.18 && x<=w*.82;
    if(hand) return y<=h*.20 && x>=w*.14 && x<=w*.86;
    if(upperArm || lowerArm || upperLeg) return (y<=h*.14 || y>=h*.86) && x>=w*.08 && x<=w*.92;
    // Shin/foot artwork keeps its shoe outline; only the knee end is a joining edge.
    if(lowerLeg) return y<=h*.15 && x>=w*.08 && x<=w*.92;
    return false;
  };
  const lum=(i)=>.2126*original[i]+.7152*original[i+1]+.0722*original[i+2];
  for(let y=1;y<h-1;y++) for(let x=1;x<w-1;x++){
    const i=(y*w+x)*4; if(original[i+3]<80 || !inJointBand(x,y)) continue;
    const l=lum(i); if(l>185) continue;
    let rr=0,gg=0,bb=0,n=0,maxL=0;
    for(let oy=-1;oy<=1;oy++) for(let ox=-1;ox<=1;ox++){
      if(!ox&&!oy) continue; const j=((y+oy)*w+x+ox)*4; if(original[j+3]<90) continue;
      const lj=lum(j); if(lj>maxL) maxL=lj;
      if(lj>l+4){ rr+=original[j]; gg+=original[j+1]; bb+=original[j+2]; n++; }
    }
    // Only soften a dark pixel that is clearly a contour against a lighter interior fill.
    if(n && maxL>l+6 && l < maxL*.97){ d[i]=Math.round(rr/n); d[i+1]=Math.round(gg/n); d[i+2]=Math.round(bb/n); cleanedPixels++; }
  }
  // Flatten mild skin/fabric shading in the joint overlap itself. Several generated sheets draw a
  // brown/black wrist crease or dark cuff-shadow a few pixels INSIDE the crop, not just on its edge,
  // so edge-only decontouring cannot remove it. Sample each material from the interior and reuse its
  // own median colour just in those tiny hidden zones; silhouette alpha and all non-joint pixels stay.
  const samples={skin:[[],[],[]],blue:[[],[],[]],gray:[[],[],[]],other:[[],[],[]]};
  for(let y=2;y<h-2;y++) for(let x=2;x<w-2;x++){
    const i=(y*w+x)*4; if(original[i+3]<150 || inJointBand(x,y)) continue;
    const r=original[i],gg=original[i+1],b=original[i+2],mx=Math.max(r,gg,b),mn=Math.min(r,gg,b);
    let cls=null;
    if(r>135 && gg>70 && r>gg*1.12 && gg>b*1.12 && r-b>28) cls='skin';
    else if(b>r*1.08 && b>gg*1.02 && b-gg>12) cls='blue';
    else if(mx<135 && mx-mn<55) cls='gray';
    else cls='other';
    if(cls) for(let c=0;c<3;c++) samples[cls][c].push(original[i+c]);
  }
  const median=arr=>{ if(arr.length<12) return null; arr.sort((a,b)=>a-b); return arr[Math.floor(arr.length/2)]; };
  const palette={}; Object.keys(samples).forEach(k=>{ palette[k]=samples[k].map(median); if(palette[k].some(v=>v===null)) palette[k]=null; });
  for(let y=0;y<h;y++) for(let x=0;x<w;x++){
    if(!inJointBand(x,y)) continue; const i=(y*w+x)*4; if(original[i+3]<45) continue;
    const r=original[i],gg=original[i+1],b=original[i+2],mx=Math.max(r,gg,b),mn=Math.min(r,gg,b); let cls=null;
    if(r>125 && gg>65 && r>gg*1.10 && gg>b*1.10 && r-b>28) cls='skin';
    else if(b>r*1.06 && b>gg*.99 && b-gg>8) cls='blue';
    else if(mx<140 && mx-mn<62) cls='gray';
    const col=cls&&palette[cls]; if(!col) continue;
    for(let c=0;c<3;c++) d[i+c]=Math.round(original[i+c]*.08+col[c]*.92);
    cleanedPixels++;
  }
  ctx.putImageData(im,0,0);
  palette.cleanedPixels=cleanedPixels;
  return palette;
}
// One cropped part -> "art": pixels + hit mask + default pivot (the joint it rotates around) + a few measurements.
function edMakeArt(canvas, key, modelType){
  const jointColors = edCleanJointEdge(canvas,key,modelType);
  const w = canvas.width, h = canvas.height, d = canvas.getContext('2d').getImageData(0,0,w,h).data, alpha = new Uint8Array(w*h);
  for(let i=0;i<w*h;i++) alpha[i] = d[i*4+3] > 30 ? 1 : 0;
  const cx = (a,b)=>{ let s = 0, n = 0; for(let y = Math.floor(a); y < Math.min(h, Math.ceil(b)); y++) for(let x = 0; x < w; x++) if(alpha[y*w+x]){ s += x; n++; } return n ? s/n : w/2; };
  let skinTop = -1;                         // first row that is mostly skin-coloured (where a sleeve ends and the bare forearm begins)
  for(let y = 0; y < h && skinTop < 0; y++){ let sk = 0, op = 0; for(let x = 0; x < w; x++){ const i = (y*w+x)*4; if(d[i+3] > 30){ op++; const r = d[i], g = d[i+1], b = d[i+2]; if(r > 120 && r > g && g > b && r-b > 35) sk++; } } if(op > 3 && sk/op > 0.5) skinTop = y; }
  let dpx, dpy;
  if(/^head/.test(key)){ dpx = cx(h*0.88, h); dpy = h*0.97; }
  else if(key === 'torso'){ dpx = w/2; dpy = h*0.9; }
  else if(isMythicModelType(modelType) && /^tail(?:_mid|_tip)?$/.test(key)){ dpx = cx(0,Math.max(2,h*0.06)); dpy = h*0.035; }   // v347: pivot at the real top-edge centre (works for curved tails)
  else if(key === 'tail' && (modelType === 'animal' || modelType === 'bird')){ dpx = w*0.94; dpy = h*0.5; }
  else if(key === 'tail'){ dpx = cx(0,h*0.1); dpy = h*0.04; }   // v344 beast-man tail: root at the TOP of the upright piece
  else if(key === 'mouth_closed' || key === 'mouth_open' || key === 'mouth_hold' || /^(?:mouth_inner|eye_[LR]|eye_closed_[LR]|brow_[LR])$/.test(key)){ dpx = w/2; dpy = h/2; }
  // Animal paw and bird wingtip alternatives all share a top-edge attachment point. The humanoid
  // point/thumb/palm pivots below are intentionally kept for human hand silhouettes only.
  else if((modelType === 'animal' || modelType === 'bird') && /^hand[LR](?:_(?:fist|point|thumb|palm))?$/.test(key)){ dpx = cx(0,h*0.1); dpy = h*0.06; }
  // Gesture art is not always upright: pointing hands have a wrist on the side, while thumbs-up
  // and palm pieces have the wrist at the bottom. Put the pivot on the actual wrist edge instead
  // of treating every variant like the plain open hand (this prevents detached gesture swaps).
  else if(/^handL_point$/.test(key)){ dpx = w*0.035; dpy = h*0.5; }
  else if(/^handR_point$/.test(key)){ dpx = w*0.965; dpy = h*0.5; }
  else if(/^hand[LR]_(?:thumb|palm)$/.test(key)){ dpx = cx(h*0.88,h); dpy = h*0.96; }
  else if(/^hand[LR](?:_fist)?$/.test(key)){ dpx = cx(0,h*0.1); dpy = h*0.06; }
  else { dpx = cx(0, h*0.1); dpy = h*(/lower|fist|point|thumb|palm/.test(key) ? 0.06 : 0.05); }
  return { canvas, w, h, alpha, dpx, dpy, jointColors:jointColors||{}, jointCleanedPixels:Number(jointColors&&jointColors.cleanedPixels)||0, botCx:cx(h*0.92, h), topCx:cx(0,Math.max(2,h*0.06)), topEdgeX:cx(0,2), botEdgeX:cx(Math.max(0,h-2),h), skinTop: skinTop < 0 ? -1 : skinTop/h };
}
// The new humanoid format places cut-outs around an upright anatomical silhouette (head above
// torso, arms at the sides, legs below) instead of putting every piece into a rectangular grid.
// This detector keeps legacy two-row 12-piece sheets usable by checking that the topmost piece is
// near the horizontal centre and that a separate central torso-like component sits below it.
function sheetLooksLikeRegularPartGrid(parts, w, h){
  if(!parts || parts.length < 10 || !w || !h) return false;
  const bucket = (values, tol) => {
    const out=[];
    values.slice().sort((a,b)=>a-b).forEach(v=>{
      const last=out[out.length-1];
      if(!last || Math.abs(v-last.mean)>tol) out.push({values:[v],mean:v,count:1});
      else { last.values.push(v); last.mean=last.values.reduce((a,b)=>a+b,0)/last.values.length; last.count=last.values.length; }
    });
    return out;
  };
  const xs=parts.map(b=>(b.x0+b.x1)/2), ys=parts.map(b=>(b.y0+b.y1)/2);
  const cols=bucket(xs,Math.max(3,w*.055)), rows=bucket(ys,Math.max(3,h*.035));
  if(cols.length<4 || rows.length<3) return false;
  const gapStats=clusters=>{
    const gaps=[]; for(let i=1;i<clusters.length;i++) gaps.push(clusters[i].mean-clusters[i-1].mean);
    const mean=gaps.reduce((a,b)=>a+b,0)/Math.max(1,gaps.length);
    return {mean,spread:mean?Math.max(...gaps.map(v=>Math.abs(v-mean)))/mean:Infinity,min:Math.min(...gaps)};
  };
  const gx=gapStats(cols), gy=gapStats(rows);
  // A true model-sheet grid repeats cell-centre spacing horizontally AND vertically.
  // Anatomical cut-outs may form rows, but their irregular shoulder/hand/leg x positions
  // must not be mistaken for a grid simply because some centres happen to line up.
  if(gx.spread>.38 || gy.spread>.48 || gx.min<w*.075 || gy.min<h*.035) return false;
  const repeatedCols=cols.filter(c=>c.count>=2).length;
  const repeatedRows=rows.filter(r=>r.count>=3).length;
  const strongRows=rows.filter(r=>r.count>=Math.max(2,Math.ceil(cols.length*.55))).length;
  const strongCols=cols.filter(c=>c.count>=2).length;
  return repeatedCols>=Math.ceil(cols.length*.65) && repeatedRows>=2 && strongRows>=Math.ceil(rows.length*.6) && strongCols>=Math.ceil(cols.length*.7) && parts.length/cols.length>=1.8;
}
function sheetIsAnatomy12(parts, w, h){
  if(!parts || parts.length !== 12) return false;
  const cx = b => (b.x0 + b.x1) / 2;
  const cy = b => (b.y0 + b.y1) / 2;
  const head = parts.reduce((best,b)=>cy(b)<cy(best)?b:best, parts[0]);
  const headX = cx(head) / Math.max(1,w);
  if(headX < 0.30 || headX > 0.70) return false;
  const possible = parts.filter(b=>b!==head && cx(b)/Math.max(1,w)>=0.31 && cx(b)/Math.max(1,w)<=0.69 && cy(b)>cy(head)+h*0.06 && cy(b)<h*0.67);
  if(!possible.length) return false;
  const torso = possible.sort((a,b)=>b.n-a.n)[0];
  const torsoX = cx(torso) / Math.max(1,w), torsoY = cy(torso) / Math.max(1,h), headY = cy(head) / Math.max(1,h);
  return torsoX >= 0.32 && torsoX <= 0.68 && torsoY > headY + 0.06 && torsoY < 0.68;
}
function sheetOrderAnatomy12(parts, w, h){
  const cx = b => (b.x0 + b.x1) / 2;
  const cy = b => (b.y0 + b.y1) / 2;
  const centreX = w / 2;
  const list = parts.slice();
  const head = list.reduce((best,b)=>cy(b)<cy(best)?b:best, list[0]);
  let rest = list.filter(b=>b!==head);
  const torsoPool = rest.filter(b=>cx(b) >= w*0.31 && cx(b) <= w*0.69 && cy(b) > cy(head)+h*0.05 && cy(b) < h*0.68);
  if(!torsoPool.length) return null;
  const torso = torsoPool.sort((a,b)=>b.n-a.n)[0];
  rest = rest.filter(b=>b!==torso);
  // Legs sit lower AND closer to the canvas centre than the side-positioned arms/hands.
  // Combining both signals is safer than relying on vertical position alone, because a relaxed
  // hand can hang down to hip height in an anatomy-position sheet.
  const legParts = rest.slice().sort((a,b)=>{
    const score = item => Math.abs(cx(item)-centreX)/Math.max(1,w)*1.2 - (cy(item)/Math.max(1,h))*0.4;
    return score(a)-score(b);
  }).slice(0,4);
  const armParts = rest.filter(b=>!legParts.includes(b));
  if(armParts.length !== 6 || legParts.length !== 4) return null;
  const byY = items => items.slice().sort((a,b)=>cy(a)-cy(b));
  const bySide = pair => pair.slice().sort((a,b)=>cx(a)-cx(b)); // viewer-left, then viewer-right
  const armSorted = byY(armParts);
  const upperArms = bySide(armSorted.slice(0,2));
  const forearms = bySide(armSorted.slice(2,4));
  const hands = bySide(armSorted.slice(4,6));
  const legSorted = byY(legParts);
  const thighs = bySide(legSorted.slice(0,2));
  const shins = bySide(legSorted.slice(2,4));
  if([upperArms,forearms,hands,thighs,shins].some(pair=>pair.length!==2)) return null;
  return [head, torso, upperArms[0], forearms[0], hands[0], upperArms[1], forearms[1], hands[1], thighs[0], shins[0], thighs[1], shins[1]];
}

// Detect a humanoid sheet arranged around one standing figure, rather than in a fixed grid.
// Several image generators let the head/neck/t-shirt edges touch, or let a wrist connect to a hand.
// These helpers use each component's position relative to the whole figure and can split those
// fused regions at the visible anatomical boundary before assigning editor part names.
function sheetFigureMetrics(parts){
  const x0=Math.min(...parts.map(b=>b.x0)), y0=Math.min(...parts.map(b=>b.y0));
  const x1=Math.max(...parts.map(b=>b.x1)), y1=Math.max(...parts.map(b=>b.y1));
  const w=Math.max(1,x1-x0+1), h=Math.max(1,y1-y0+1), mid=x0+w/2;
  const cx=b=>(b.x0+b.x1)/2, cy=b=>(b.y0+b.y1)/2;
  return {x0,y0,x1,y1,w,h,mid,cx,cy,yr:b=>(cy(b)-y0)/h,dx:b=>Math.abs(cx(b)-mid)/w,bw:b=>b.x1-b.x0+1,bh:b=>b.y1-b.y0+1};
}
function sheetOrderAnatomy14(parts,w,h){
  if(!parts || parts.length!==14) return null;
  const M=sheetFigureMetrics(parts);
  if(M.h < M.w*1.2) return null;
  const leftOf=(a,b)=>M.cx(a)-M.cx(b);
  // A genuine anatomy-position sheet has a small top-centre head and a separate, broader torso.
  const headPool=parts.filter(b=>b.y0<=M.y0+M.h*.20 && M.yr(b)<.22 && M.dx(b)<.19 && M.bh(b)<M.h*.34);
  if(!headPool.length) return null;
  const head=headPool.sort((a,b)=>a.y0-b.y0 || b.n-a.n)[0];
  const torsoPool=parts.filter(b=>b!==head && M.yr(b)>.19 && M.yr(b)<.58 && M.dx(b)<.20 && M.bh(b)>M.h*.12);
  if(!torsoPool.length) return null;
  const torso=torsoPool.sort((a,b)=>b.n-a.n)[0];
  const used=new Set([head,torso]);
  const upper=parts.filter(b=>!used.has(b) && M.yr(b)>.12 && M.yr(b)<.42 && M.dx(b)>.18).sort(leftOf);
  if(upper.length!==2) return null;
  upper.forEach(b=>used.add(b));
  const shoes=parts.filter(b=>!used.has(b) && M.yr(b)>.89 && b.y0>M.y0+M.h*.83).sort(leftOf);
  if(shoes.length!==2) return null; shoes.forEach(b=>used.add(b));
  const shins=parts.filter(b=>!used.has(b) && M.yr(b)>.66 && M.yr(b)<.91 && M.dx(b)<.31 && b.y0>M.y0+M.h*.58).sort(leftOf);
  if(shins.length!==2) return null; shins.forEach(b=>used.add(b));
  const thighs=parts.filter(b=>!used.has(b) && M.yr(b)>.42 && M.yr(b)<.70 && M.dx(b)<.25 && b.y0>M.y0+M.h*.35).sort(leftOf);
  if(thighs.length!==2) return null; thighs.forEach(b=>used.add(b));
  const lower=parts.filter(b=>!used.has(b) && M.yr(b)>.31 && M.yr(b)<.68 && M.dx(b)>.17);
  if(lower.length!==4) return null;
  const l=lower.filter(b=>M.cx(b)<M.mid).sort((a,b)=>M.cy(a)-M.cy(b));
  const r=lower.filter(b=>M.cx(b)>=M.mid).sort((a,b)=>M.cy(a)-M.cy(b));
  if(l.length!==2 || r.length!==2) return null;
  // Key order is deliberately the same as SHEET_KEYS_14; L/R means viewer-left/viewer-right.
  return [head,torso,upper[0],upper[1],l[0],l[1],r[0],r[1],thighs[0],thighs[1],shins[0],shins[1],shoes[0],shoes[1]];
}
// Fairy and Devil sheets add exactly two detached back wings to the normal 14-part humanoid anatomy.
// Find the balanced left/right wing pair whose removal leaves a valid 14-part humanoid layout.
function sheetOrderWingedHumanoid16(parts,w,h){
  if(!parts||parts.length!==16)return null;
  const M=sheetFigureMetrics(parts);
  if(M.h<M.w*.78)return null;
  let best=null,bestScore=Infinity;
  for(let i=0;i<parts.length;i++)for(let j=i+1;j<parts.length;j++){
    const a=parts[i],b=parts[j],left=M.cx(a)<M.mid?a:b,right=left===a?b:a;
    if(!(M.cx(left)<M.mid&&M.cx(right)>M.mid))continue;
    const yGap=Math.abs(M.cy(left)-M.cy(right))/M.h;
    const mirrorGap=Math.abs((M.mid-M.cx(left))-(M.cx(right)-M.mid))/M.w;
    const level=(M.yr(left)+M.yr(right))*.5;
    const areaA=Math.max(1,M.bw(left)*M.bh(left)),areaB=Math.max(1,M.bw(right)*M.bh(right));
    const areaGap=Math.abs(Math.log(areaA/areaB));
    if(yGap>.16||mirrorGap>.19||level<.15||level>.68||areaGap>1.25)continue;
    const rest=parts.filter((_,idx)=>idx!==i&&idx!==j);
    const body=sheetOrderAnatomy14(rest,w,h)||sheetOrderAnatomy14ByPosition(rest,w,h);
    if(!body)continue;
    const outer=(M.dx(left)+M.dx(right))/2,size=Math.sqrt(areaA*areaB)/Math.max(1,M.w*M.h);const score=mirrorGap*2+yGap+areaGap*.035+Math.abs(level-.39)*.12-outer*.3-Math.min(.12,size)*2.2;   // v347: wings = outermost, largest mirrored pair
    if(score<bestScore){bestScore=score;best=[...body,left,right];}
  }
  return best;
}

// Positional fallback for anatomy-position sheets generated with slightly different spacing.
// The six central/lower pieces are ranked into thighs, shins and shoes; the remaining six are
// grouped by viewer-left/viewer-right and ordered from shoulder to hand. This avoids assigning a
// forearm or sleeve to Head/Hand merely because an AI placed it a little lower than the template.
function sheetOrderAnatomy14ByPosition(parts,w,h){
  if(!parts || parts.length!==14) return null;
  const M=sheetFigureMetrics(parts);
  if(M.h < M.w*.82) return null;
  const scoreHead=b=>M.yr(b)*1.0 + M.dx(b)*1.15 + Math.max(0,M.bh(b)/M.h-.22)*.15;
  const headPool=parts.filter(b=>M.yr(b)<.36 && M.dx(b)<.28);
  if(!headPool.length) return null;
  const head=headPool.slice().sort((a,b)=>scoreHead(a)-scoreHead(b))[0];
  const torsoPool=parts.filter(b=>b!==head && M.yr(b)>.12 && M.yr(b)<.68 && M.dx(b)<.25 && M.bw(b)>M.bh(b)*.38);
  if(!torsoPool.length) return null;
  const torso=torsoPool.slice().sort((a,b)=>{
    const sc=x=>Math.sqrt(Math.max(1,x.n))*2 + M.bw(x)*.6 + M.bh(x)*.15 - M.dx(x)*M.w*.8 - Math.abs(M.yr(x)-.43)*M.h*.08;
    return sc(b)-sc(a);
  })[0];
  const rest=parts.filter(b=>b!==head && b!==torso);
  const lower=rest.filter(b=>M.yr(b)>.34);
  if(lower.length<6) return null;
  // Leg parts sit closer to the centre line; the y bias separates them from hands which hang farther out.
  const legScore=b=>M.dx(b) + Math.max(0,.55-M.yr(b))*.42;
  let legs=lower.slice().sort((a,b)=>legScore(a)-legScore(b)).slice(0,6);
  if(legs.length!==6) return null;
  const orderedLegs=legs.slice().sort((a,b)=>M.cy(a)-M.cy(b));
  const legsLeftToRight=(pair)=>pair.slice().sort((a,b)=>M.cx(a)-M.cx(b));
  const thighs=legsLeftToRight(orderedLegs.slice(0,2));
  const shins=legsLeftToRight(orderedLegs.slice(2,4));
  const shoes=legsLeftToRight(orderedLegs.slice(4,6));
  const armParts=rest.filter(b=>!legs.includes(b));
  if(armParts.length!==6) return null;
  let left=armParts.filter(b=>M.cx(b)<M.mid), right=armParts.filter(b=>M.cx(b)>=M.mid);
  if(left.length!==3 || right.length!==3) return null;
  left=left.sort((a,b)=>M.cy(a)-M.cy(b)); right=right.sort((a,b)=>M.cy(a)-M.cy(b));
